Implement the following plan:

# TA-307: Snowflake リソースを Pulumi で管理する

## Context

既存の Pulumi プロジェクト（Go）は Cloudflare リソース（D1, R2, KV）を管理している。Snowflake をデータウェアハウスとして活用するため、同じ Pulumi プロジェクトに Snowflake プロバイダーを追加し、基盤リソース（Database, Schema, Warehouse）を IaC で管理する。Phase 1 として基本リソースのみ対応。

## 変更対象ファイル

| ファイル | 変更内容 |
|---------|---------|
| `infrastructure/pulumi/go.mod` | `pulumi-snowflake/sdk/v2` 依存追加 |
| `infrastructure/pulumi/main.go` | Snowflake リソース定義追加（Database, Schema, Warehouse） |
| `.github/workflows/pulumi-preview.yml` | Snowflake 認証用環境変数追加 |
| `.github/workflows/pulumi-deploy.yml` | Snowflake 認証用環境変数追加 |

## 実装手順

### Step 1: ブランチ作成

```bash
git checkout main && git pull
git checkout -b feat/add-snowflake-provider
```

### Step 2: Snowflake SDK 依存追加

```bash
cd infrastructure/pulumi
go get github.com/pulumi/pulumi-snowflake/sdk/v2
go mod tidy
```

### Step 3: main.go に Snowflake リソース追加

既存のコードパターン（コメントブロック区切り、error-return-early、ctx.Export）に準拠して以下を追加:

- **import**: `"github.com/pulumi/pulumi-snowflake/sdk/v2/go/snowflake"` 追加
- **Database**: `RAW` - データ取り込み用
- **Schema**: `RAW.INGESTION` - sfDatabase.Name を参照して依存関係を明示
- **Warehouse**: `ANALYTICS_WH` - XSMALL, auto_suspend=60s, auto_resume=true
- **Outputs**: `snowflakeDatabaseName`, `snowflakeSchemaName`, `snowflakeWarehouseName`

認証方式: 環境変数による暗黙的プロバイダー構成（Cloudflare プロバイダーと同じパターン）

### Step 4: CI/CD ワークフロー更新

両ワークフローの `env:` ブロックに以下を追加:

```yaml
SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME }}
SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME }}
SNOWFLAKE_USER: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_USER }}
SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY }}
SNOWFLAKE_ROLE: SYSADMIN
```

### Step 5: ビルド確認

```bash
cd infrastructure/pulumi && go build ./...
```

## 設計判断

- **認証**: JWT (Private Key) - 環境変数で注入。CI/CD との親和性が高い
- **ロール**: `SYSADMIN` - DB/Schema/Warehouse 作成に必要十分な権限
- **Pulumi logical name**: Cloudflare の `"raw"` と Snowflake の `"raw"` は URN にリソースタイプが含まれるため衝突しない
- **`AutoResume`**: Snowflake Provider v2 では `pulumi.StringPtrInput` 型 → `pulumi.String("true")` を使用

## ユーザー手動作業（PR マージ後）

1. GitHub Secrets に `SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME`, `SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME`, `SNOWFLAKE_USER`, `SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY` を追加
2. Snowflake 側で RSA キーペア認証を設定
3. ローカルの `.env.local` にも Snowflake 環境変数を追加

## 検証方法

1. `go build ./...` でコンパイル確認
2. ローカルで `pulumi preview` 実行 → 3 リソース（Database, Schema, Warehouse）の create が表示されること
3. PR 作成後、CI の Pulumi Preview が正常に実行されること

```
     1→name: Pulumi Preview
     2→
     3→on:
     4→  pull_request:
     5→    paths:
     6→      - "infrastructure/pulumi/**"
     7→      - ".github/workflows/pulumi-preview.yml"
     8→
     9→env:
    10→  CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN }}
    11→  CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID }}
    12→  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: ${{ secrets.R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID }}
    13→  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: ${{ secrets.R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY }}
    14→  PULUMI_CONFIG_PASSPHRASE: ${{ secrets.PULUMI_CONFIG_PASSPHRASE }}
    15→
    16→jobs:
    17→  preview:
    18→    name: Preview
    19→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    20→    permissions:
    21→      contents: read
    22→      pull-requests: write
    23→    defaults:
    24→      run:
    25→        working-directory: infrastructure/pulumi
    26→
    27→    steps:
    28→      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
    29→
    30→      - uses: actions/setup-go@v5
    31→        with:
    32→          go-version: "1.24"
    33→          cache-dependency-path: infrastructure/pulumi/go.sum
    34→
    35→      - name: Install dependencies
    36→        run: go mod tidy
    37→
    38→      - name: Login to R2 backend
    39→        run: pulumi login 's3://pulumi?endpoint=https://${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID }}.r2.cloudflarestorage.com&region=auto&s3ForcePathStyle=true'
    40→
    41→      - name: Select stack
    42→        run: pulumi stack select dev
    43→
    44→      - name: Pulumi Preview
    45→        id: preview
    46→        run: |
    47→          set +e
    48→
    49→          # Run preview with JSON output for parsing
    50→          pulumi preview --json 2>/dev/null > preview.json
    51→          JSON_EXIT=$?
    52→
    53→          # Also get human-readable output
    54→          pulumi preview --diff 2>&1 | tee preview.txt
    55→          PREVIEW_EXIT=${PIPESTATUS[0]}
    56→
    57→          echo "exit_code=$PREVIEW_EXIT" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    58→
    59→          # Parse JSON for accurate counts
    60→          if [ -f preview.json ] && [ -s preview.json ]; then
    61→            CREATES=$(jq '[.steps[]? | select(.op == "create")] | length' preview.json 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    62→            UPDATES=$(jq '[.steps[]? | select(.op == "update")] | length' preview.json 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    63→            DELETES=$(jq '[.steps[]? | select(.op == "delete")] | length' preview.json 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    64→            SAMES=$(jq '[.steps[]? | select(.op == "same")] | length' preview.json 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    65→          else
    66→            # Fallback to text parsing
    67→            CREATES=$(grep -c "^\s*+.*create" preview.txt 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    68→            UPDATES=$(grep -c "^\s*~.*update" preview.txt 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    69→            DELETES=$(grep -c "^\s*-.*delete" preview.txt 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    70→            SAMES=$(grep -oP '\d+(?= unchanged)' preview.txt 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    71→          fi
    72→
    73→          echo "creates=$CREATES" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    74→          echo "updates=$UPDATES" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    75→          echo "deletes=$DELETES" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    76→          echo "sames=$SAMES" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    77→
    78→          # Determine result status
    79→          TOTAL_CHANGES=$((CREATES + UPDATES + DELETES))
    80→          if [ $PREVIEW_EXIT -ne 0 ]; then
    81→            echo "result=failed" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    82→          elif [ $TOTAL_CHANGES -eq 0 ]; then
    83→            echo "result=no-changes" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    84→          else
    85→            echo "result=has-changes" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    86→          fi
    87→
    88→      - name: Generate PR Comment
    89→        id: comment
    90→        run: |
    91→          RESULT="${{ steps.preview.outputs.result }}"
    92→          CREATES="${{ steps.preview.outputs.creates }}"
    93→          UPDATES="${{ steps.preview.outputs.updates }}"
    94→          DELETES="${{ steps.preview.outputs.deletes }}"
    95→          SAMES="${{ steps.preview.outputs.sames }}"
    96→
    97→          # Set header based on result
    98→          case $RESULT in
    99→            "failed")
   100→              HEADER="## ❌ Pulumi Preview Failed"
   101→              BADGE="https://img.shields.io/badge/pulumi-failed-red?style=flat-square&logo=pulumi"
   102→              ;;
   103→            "no-changes")
   104→              HEADER="## ✅ Pulumi Preview: No Changes"
   105→              BADGE="https://img.shields.io/badge/pulumi-no%20changes-green?style=flat-square&logo=pulumi"
   106→              ;;
   107→            "has-changes")
   108→              HEADER="## 🔄 Pulumi Preview: Changes Detected"
   109→              BADGE="https://img.shields.io/badge/pulumi-changes%20detected-blue?style=flat-square&logo=pulumi"
   110→              ;;
   111→          esac
   112→
   113→          # Build comment
   114→          cat << 'COMMENT_EOF' > comment.md
   115→          HEADER_PLACEHOLDER
   116→
   117→          ![Pulumi](BADGE_PLACEHOLDER)
   118→
   119→          ### 📊 Resource Changes
   120→
   121→          | | Create | Update | Delete | Unchanged |
   122→          |:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|
   123→          | Count | **CREATES_PLACEHOLDER** | **UPDATES_PLACEHOLDER** | **DELETES_PLACEHOLDER** | SAMES_PLACEHOLDER |
   124→          | | 🆕 | 📝 | 🗑️ | ⏸️ |
   125→
   126→          COMMENT_EOF
   127→
   128→          # Add warning section for destructive changes
   129→          if [ "$DELETES" -gt 0 ]; then
   130→            cat << 'WARNING_EOF' >> comment.md
   131→
   132→          > [!CAUTION]
   133→          > **DELETES_PLACEHOLDER resources will be deleted.** Please review carefully before merging.
   134→
   135→          WARNING_EOF
   136→          fi
   137→
   138→          # Add details section
   139→          cat << 'DETAILS_EOF' >> comment.md
   140→
   141→          <details>
   142→          <summary>📋 Preview Details</summary>
   143→
   144→          ```diff
   145→          DETAILS_EOF
   146→
   147→          # Add preview output (truncated if needed)
   148→          head -c 60000 preview.txt >> comment.md
   149→
   150→          cat << 'END_EOF' >> comment.md
   151→          ```
   152→
   153→          </details>
   154→
   155→          ---
   156→
   157→          <sub>🏗️ **Stack:** `dev` | 📁 **Path:** `infrastructure/pulumi` | 🔗 **Workflow:** [Run #${{ github.run_number }}](${{ github.server_url }}/${{ github.repository }}/actions/runs/${{ github.run_id }})</sub>
   158→          END_EOF
   159→
   160→          # Replace placeholders
   161→          sed -i "s|HEADER_PLACEHOLDER|$HEADER|g" comment.md
   162→          sed -i "s|BADGE_PLACEHOLDER|$BADGE|g" comment.md
   163→          sed -i "s|CREATES_PLACEHOLDER|$CREATES|g" comment.md
   164→          sed -i "s|UPDATES_PLACEHOLDER|$UPDATES|g" comment.md
   165→          sed -i "s|DELETES_PLACEHOLDER|$DELETES|g" comment.md
   166→          sed -i "s|SAMES_PLACEHOLDER|$SAMES|g" comment.md
   167→
   168→      - name: Post PR Comment
   169→        uses: actions/github-script@v7
   170→        with:
   171→          script: |
   172→            const fs = require('fs');
   173→            const body = fs.readFileSync('infrastructure/pulumi/comment.md', 'utf8');
   174→
   175→            const marker = '<!-- pulumi-preview-comment -->';
   176→            const fullBody = marker + '\n' + body;
   177→
   178→            // Find existing comment
   179→            const { data: comments } = await github.rest.issues.listComments({
   180→              owner: context.repo.owner,
   181→              repo: context.repo.repo,
   182→              issue_number: context.issue.number,
   183→            });
   184→
   185→            const existingComment = comments.find(c => c.body.includes(marker));
   186→
   187→            if (existingComment) {
   188→              await github.rest.issues.updateComment({
   189→                owner: context.repo.owner,
   190→                repo: context.repo.repo,
   191→                comment_id: existingComment.id,
   192→                body: fullBody
   193→              });
   194→              console.log('Updated existing comment');
   195→            } else {
   196→              await github.rest.issues.createComment({
   197→                owner: context.repo.owner,
   198→                repo: context.repo.repo,
   199→                issue_number: context.issue.number,
   200→                body: fullBody
   201→              });
   202→              console.log('Created new comment');

  "content": "package main\n\nimport (\n\t\"github.com/pulumi/pulumi-cloudflare/sdk/v5/go/cloudflare\"\n\t\"github.com/pulumi/pulumi/sdk/v3/go/pulumi\"\n)\n\ntype CloudflareOutputs struct {\n\tD1DatabaseId   pulumi.IDOutput\n\tD1DatabaseName pulumi.StringOutput\n\tR2BucketName   pulumi.StringOutput\n\tKvNamespaceId  pulumi.IDOutput\n}\n\nfunc createCloudflareResources(ctx *pulumi.Context, accountId string) (*CloudflareOutputs, error) {\n\t// ===========================================\n\t// D1 Database\n\t// ===========================================\n\t// 既存のD1データベースをインポートして管理\n\t// pulumi import cloudflare:index/d1Database:D1Database raw <account_id>/<database_id>\n\trawDb, err := cloudflare.NewD1Database(ctx, \"raw\", &cloudflare.D1DatabaseArgs{\n\t\tAccountId: pulumi.String(accountId),\n\t\tName:      pulumi.String(\"raw\"),\n\t}, pulumi.Protect(true))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\t// ===========================================\n\t// R2 Bucket (for data lake)\n\t// ===========================================\n\tdataLake, err := cloudflare.NewR2Bucket(ctx, \"data-lake\", &cloudflare.R2BucketArgs{\n\t\tAccountId: pulumi.String(accountId),\n\t\tName:      pulumi.String(\"data-lake\"),\n\t\tLocation:  pulumi.String(\"APAC\"),\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\t// ===========================================\n\t// Workers KV Namespace (for caching)\n\t// ===========================================\n\tcacheKv, err := cloudflare.NewWorkersKvNamespace(ctx, \"data-cache\", &cloudflare.WorkersKvNamespaceArgs{\n\t\tAccountId: pulumi.String(accountId),\n\t\tTitle:     pulumi.String(\"data-cache\"),\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\treturn &CloudflareOutputs{\n\t\tD1DatabaseId:   rawDb.ID(),\n\t\tD1DatabaseName: rawDb.Name,\n\t\tR2BucketName:   dataLake.Name,\n\t\tKvNamespaceId:  cacheKv.ID(),\n\t}, nil\n}\n"
}

}

> AGENT

Pulumi で Snowflake のロールやユーザーも管理するのは良いですね。どういった構成をお考えですか？

例えばこんなパターンが考えられます:

**ロール**
- `INGESTION_ROLE` - データ取り込み用（Workers からの書き込み）
- `TRANSFORM_ROLE` - dbt 変換用
- `ANALYTICS_ROLE` - 分析/読み取り用

**ユーザー**
- `INGESTION_SVC` - ingestion Worker 用サービスアカウント
- `DBT_SVC` - dbt 用サービスアカウント

**権限付与**
- 各ロールに対する DB/Schema/Warehouse の権限
- 各ユーザーへのロール割り当て

  "description": "RSA キーペア生成（Snowflake 認証用）"
}

> AGENT

キーペアを生成しました。以下の SQL を Snowsight で実行してください:

```sql
-- ============================================
-- 1. Pulumi 用ウェアハウス（リソース管理用、最小サイズ）
-- ============================================
USE ROLE SYSADMIN;

CREATE WAREHOUSE IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_WH
  WAREHOUSE_SIZE = 'XSMALL'
  AUTO_SUSPEND = 60
  AUTO_RESUME = TRUE
  INITIALLY_SUSPENDED = TRUE
  COMMENT = 'Warehouse for Pulumi IaC operations';

-- ============================================
-- 2. Pulumi 用ロール
-- ============================================
USE ROLE USERADMIN;

CREATE ROLE IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_ROLE
  COMMENT = 'Role for Pulumi infrastructure management';

-- SYSADMIN 配下にロールを配置
GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO ROLE SYSADMIN;

-- ============================================
-- 3. Pulumi 用サービスユーザー
-- ============================================
CREATE USER IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_SVC
  TYPE = SERVICE
  DEFAULT_ROLE = PULUMI_ROLE
  DEFAULT_WAREHOUSE = PULUMI_WH
  RSA_PUBLIC_KEY = 'REDACTED'
  COMMENT = 'Service user for Pulumi IaC';

GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO USER PULUMI_SVC;

-- ============================================
-- 4. 権限付与（Pulumi が DB/Schema/WH を作成できるように）
-- ============================================
USE ROLE SECURITYADMIN;

GRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE WAREHOUSE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;

-- ウェアハウス使用権限
GRANT USAGE ON WAREHOUSE PULUMI_WH TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
```

実行後に設定が必要な値:

| 設定先 | キー | 値 |
|--------|------|-----|
| GitHub Secrets | `SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME` | Snowflake の org 名 |
| GitHub Secrets | `SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME` | アカウント名 |
| GitHub Secrets | `SNOWFLAKE_USER` | `PULUMI_SVC` |
| GitHub Secrets | `SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY` | 上記の Private Key |
| `.env.local` | 同上 | 同上 |

秘密鍵は `/tmp/snowflake_pulumi_key.p8` に保存されています。`.env.local` に設定したら `/tmp` のファイルは削除してください。

Snowsight での実行が完了したら教えてください。CI ワークフローの `SNOWFLAKE_ROLE` を `PULUMI_ROLE` に更新します。

### Authenticate using Programmatic Access Token as Password

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/user-guide/programmatic-access-tokens

Demonstrates how to use a programmatic access token as the password when connecting to Snowflake, applicable to drivers and third-party applications.

```APIDOC
## Authenticate with Programmatic Access Token as Password

### Description
Use the generated programmatic access token as the password when connecting to Snowflake. This applies to driver connection settings and third-party applications.

### Method
Connection/Authentication

### Endpoint
N/A (Driver/Application Specific)

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
None

### Request Example (Snowflake Connector for Python)
```python
import snowflake.connector

conn = snowflake.connector.connect(
    user=USER,
    password=<programmatic_access_token>,
    account=ACCOUNT,
    warehouse=WAREHOUSE,
    database=DATABASE,
    schema=SCHEMA
)
```

### Response
#### Success Response
Successful connection to Snowflake.

#### Response Example
N/A (Connection established)
```

--------------------------------

### Programmatic Access Token (PAT)

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/odbc/odbc-parameters

A Snowflake-specific authentication method for non-interactive access. Requires prior account enablement.

```APIDOC
## Programmatic Access Token (PAT)

### Description
Programmatic Access Tokens (PATs) offer a Snowflake-native method for authentication without human interaction. This feature must be enabled for the account prior to use.

### Method
Connection Parameter Configuration

### Endpoint
N/A (Configuration-based)

### Parameters
#### Connection Parameters
- **authenticator** (string) - Required - Set to `SNOWFLAKE_PAT`.
- **token** (string) - Required - The generated Programmatic Access Token.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "authenticator": "SNOWFLAKE_PAT",
  "token=[REDACTED]"
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (Connection Established)
- **status** (string) - Indicates successful connection.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "status": "connected"
}
```
```

### Authenticate to Snowflake Endpoint using Programmatic Access Token

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/user-guide/programmatic-access-tokens

Details the HTTP headers required to authenticate to Snowflake REST API or SQL API endpoints using a programmatic access token.

```APIDOC
## Authenticate to Snowflake Endpoint with Programmatic Access Token

### Description
Authenticate to Snowflake endpoints by including specific HTTP headers in your request. The `Authorization` header is mandatory, and `X-Snowflake-Authorization-Token-Type` is optional.

### Method
HTTP Request (e.g., GET, POST)

### Endpoint
Snowflake API Endpoints (e.g., REST API, SQL API)

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
Depends on the specific API endpoint.

### Request Example (cURL to Snowflake REST API)
```bash
curl --location "https://myorganization-myaccount.snowflakecomputing.com/api/v2/databases" \
  --header "Authorization: Bearer <token_secret>"
```

### Request Example (cURL to Snowflake SQL API)
```bash
curl -si -X POST https://myorganization-myaccount.snowflakecomputing.com/api/v2/statements \
  --header "Content-Type: application/json" \
  --header "Accept: application/json" \
  --header "Authorization: Bearer <token_secret>" \
  --data '{"statement": "select 1"}'
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200 OK)
Depends on the specific API endpoint.

#### Response Example
Depends on the specific API endpoint.
```

### Get Access Token with cURL for External OAuth

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/user-guide/oauth-ext-custom

This command demonstrates how to obtain an access token from an Identity Provider (IdP) using cURL for External OAuth authentication with Snowflake. It requires specifying the client ID, client secret, user credentials, grant type, desired scope, and the IdP's token endpoint.

```shell
curl -X POST -H "Content-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded;charset=UTF-8" \
  --user <OAUTH_CLIENT_ID>:<OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET> \
  --data-urlencode "username=<IdP_USER_USERNAME>" \
  --data-urlencode "password=<IdP_USER_PASSWORD>" \
  --data-urlencode "grant_type=password" \
  --data-urlencode "scope=session:role:analyst" \
  <IdP_TOKEN_ENDPOINT>
```

### Use workload identity federation (WIF) OpenID Connect (OIDC) authentication

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/snowflake-cli/cicd/integrate-ci-cd

Workload identity federation (WIF) OpenID Connect (OIDC) authentication provides a secure and modern way to authenticate with Snowflake without storing private keys as secrets. This approach uses GitHub’s OIDC (OpenID Connect) token to authenticate with Snowflake. To set up WIF OIDC authentication, you need to configure WIF OIDC by setting up a service user with the OIDC workload identity type, store your Snowflake account identifier in GitHub secrets, and configure the Snowflake CLI action in your GitHub workflow YAML file.
```

> AGENT

Snowflake は OIDC 対応しています。具体的には **Workload Identity Federation (WIF)** として GitHub Actions の OIDC トークンで直接認証できます。

これは RSA キーペアよりもセキュアです:

| 方式 | メリット | デメリット |
|------|---------|-----------|
| **RSA キーペア** | 設定がシンプル | 秘密鍵を GitHub Secrets に保存する必要がある |
| **WIF OIDC** | シークレット不要、トークンは短命 | Snowflake 側の設定が少し多い |

もう少し詳しく確認します。

### Create OIDC-based Snowflake Service User for GitHub Actions

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/user-guide/tutorials/dbt-projects-on-snowflake-ci-cd-tutorial

This SQL snippet demonstrates how to create a service user in Snowflake for GitHub Actions using OpenID Connect (OIDC). This method is recommended for enhanced security as it uses short-lived tokens instead of long-lived credentials. The `SUBJECT` should uniquely identify your repository and environment.

```sql
CREATE USER IF NOT EXISTS github_actions_service_user
  TYPE = SERVICE
  WORKLOAD_IDENTITY = (
    TYPE = OIDC
    ISSUER = 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com',
    SUBJECT = 'repo:your_repo_org/your_dbt_repo:environment:prod'
  )
  DEFAULT_ROLE = <role_name>
  COMMENT = 'Service user for GitHub Actions';

```

--------------------------------

### Configure Snowflake WIF OIDC Authentication (SQL)

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/snowflake-cli/cicd/integrate-ci-cd

SQL statement to create a Snowflake service user with OIDC workload identity type. Requires specifying the OIDC issuer and subject, which is typically derived from GitHub repository and environment details.

```sql
CREATE USER <username>
TYPE = SERVICE
WORKLOAD_IDENTITY = (
  TYPE = OIDC
  ISSUER = 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com'
  SUBJECT = '<your_subject>'
)

```

--------------------------------

### Configure Snowflake CLI Action for WIF OIDC (YAML)

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/snowflake-cli/cicd/integrate-ci-cd

GitHub Actions workflow YAML configuration to set up the Snowflake CLI for WIF OIDC authentication. It requires `id-token: write` permission, checks out code, sets up the Snowflake CLI action with `use-oidc: true`, and tests the connection using secrets.

```yaml
name: Snowflake OIDC
on: [push]

permissions:
  id-token: write  # Required for OIDC token generation
  contents: read

jobs:
  oidc-job:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    environment: test-env # this should match the environment used in the subject
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          persist-credentials: false
      - name: Set up Snowflake CLI
        uses: snowflakedb/snowflake-cli-action@v2.0
        with:
          use-oidc: true
          cli-version: "3.11"
      - name: test connection
        env:
          SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT }}
        run: snow connection test -x

```

--------------------------------

### User Workload Identity Federation Configuration

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/sql-reference/sql/create-user

Configure workload identity federation for user authentication using providers like AWS, Azure, GCP, or OIDC.

```APIDOC
## ALTER USER / CREATE USER - Workload Identity Federation Configuration

### Description
Enables workload identity federation for user authentication. Specifies the identity provider (AWS, AZURE, GCP, OIDC) and associated properties like ARN or Issuer.

### Method
ALTER USER / CREATE USER

### Endpoint
N/A (Configuration within user properties)

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
- **WORKLOAD_IDENTITY** (object) - Required/Optional - Configuration for workload identity federation.
  - **TYPE** (string) - Required - The type of identity provider. Allowed values: `AWS`, `AZURE`, `GCP`, `OIDC`.
  - **ARN** (string) - Required for `TYPE=AWS`. The Amazon Resource Identifier (ARN) of the AWS user or role.
  - **ISSUER** (string) - Required for `TYPE=AZURE` and `TYPE=OIDC`. The issuer URL for the identity provider.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "WORKLOAD_IDENTITY": {
    "TYPE": "AWS",
    "ARN": "arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/MySnowflakeRole"
  }
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
Workload identity federation configuration updated successfully.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "message": "Workload identity federation configuration updated successfully."
}
```
```

### Use workload identity federation (WIF) OpenID Connect (OIDC) authentication

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/snowflake-cli/cicd/integrate-ci-cd

Workload identity federation (WIF) OpenID Connect (OIDC) authentication provides a secure and modern way to authenticate with Snowflake without storing private keys as secrets. This approach uses GitHub’s OIDC (OpenID Connect) token to authenticate with Snowflake. To set up WIF OIDC authentication, you need to configure WIF OIDC by setting up a service user with the OIDC workload identity type, store your Snowflake account identifier in GitHub secrets, and configure the Snowflake CLI action in your GitHub workflow YAML file.
```

> AGENT

**Snowflake は GitHub Actions OIDC にネイティブ対応**しています。RSA キーペアの代わりにこちらを使う方が良さそうです。

### WIF OIDC の仕組み

1. GitHub Actions が OIDC トークンを発行
2. Snowflake が GitHub の OIDC issuer を信頼
3. 秘密鍵やパスワードなしで認証完了

### Snowsight で実行する SQL（OIDC 版）

```sql
-- ============================================
-- 1. Pulumi 用ロール
-- ============================================
USE ROLE USERADMIN;

CREATE ROLE IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_ROLE
  COMMENT = 'Role for Pulumi infrastructure management';

GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO ROLE SYSADMIN;

-- ============================================
-- 2. Pulumi 用サービスユーザー（OIDC）
-- ============================================
CREATE USER IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_SVC
  TYPE = SERVICE
  WORKLOAD_IDENTITY = (
    TYPE = OIDC
    ISSUER = 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com'
    SUBJECT = 'repo:ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare:environment:prod'
  )
  DEFAULT_ROLE = PULUMI_ROLE
  COMMENT = 'Service user for Pulumi IaC (GitHub Actions OIDC)';

GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO USER PULUMI_SVC;

-- ============================================
-- 3. 権限付与
-- ============================================
USE ROLE SECURITYADMIN;

GRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE WAREHOUSE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
```

### CI ワークフローの変更点

OIDC にすると GitHub Secrets は **`SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT`（`org-account` 形式）だけ** で済みます。`SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY` が不要になります。

ワークフローには `permissions: id-token: write` と OIDC 用の認証設定が必要になります。

OIDC 方式に切り替えますか？それとも先に RSA キーペアで進めますか？

OIDCにする。

### New Snowflake API Authentication Integration Resources

Source: https://github.REDACTED.md

Introduces the newly added API authentication integration resources in the Snowflake Terraform provider, supporting different OAuth grant types like authorization code, client credentials, and JWT bearer.

```terraform
# Example for API Authentication Integration with Authorization Code Grant
resource "snowflake_api_authentication_integration_with_authorization_code_grant" "example_auth_code" {
  name = "my_auth_code_integration"
  type = "API"
  comment = "Integration for authorization code grant"

  enabled = true
  redirect_uri = "https://example.com/callback"
  client_id = "your_client_id"
  client_secret=[REDACTED]"
  token_uri = "https://token.example.com/oauth2/token"
  authorization_uri = "https://auth.example.com/oauth2/auth"
  scopes = ["openid", "profile"]
}

# Example for API Authentication Integration with Client Credentials
resource "snowflake_api_authentication_integration_with_client_credentials" "example_client_creds" {
  name = "my_client_creds_integration"
  type = "API"
  comment = "Integration for client credentials grant"

  enabled = true
  client_id = "your_client_id"
  client_secret=[REDACTED]"
  token_uri = "https://token.example.com/oauth2/token"
  token_aud = "your_token_audience"
}

# Example for API Authentication Integration with JWT Bearer
resource "snowflake_api_authentication_integration_with_jwt_bearer" "example_jwt" {
  name = "my_jwt_bearer_integration"
  type = "API"
  comment = "Integration for JWT bearer grant"

  enabled = true
  jwt_url = "https://jwt.example.com/auth"
  rsa_public_key_file = "./rsa_public_key.pem"
  token_aud = "your_token_audience"
}

```

--------------------------------

### Configure Snowflake External OAuth Integration with Terraform

Source: https://github.REDACTED.md

Manages an external OAuth integration in Snowflake. This allows Snowflake to use an external identity provider for authentication.

```Terraform
resource "snowflake_external_oauth_integration" "example" {
  name = "my_external_oauth"
  enabled = true
  external_oauth_issuer = "https://issuer.example.com"
  # ... other configurations
}
```

--------------------------------

### Snowflake External OAuth Integration Configuration

Source: https://github.com/snowflakedb/terraform-provider-snowflake/blob/main/docs/resources/external_oauth_integration.md

This snippet demonstrates the configuration of an external OAuth security integration in Snowflake using Terraform. It includes essential parameters such as enabling the integration, specifying the OAuth issuer URL, and defining how Snowflake maps access tokens to users and roles. Optional parameters for allowed/blocked roles, audience lists, and key URLs are also shown.

```Terraform
resource "snowflake_security_integration" "example" {
  name                                     = "example_oauth_integration"
  type                                     = "EXTERNAL_OAUTH"
  enabled                                  = true
  external_oauth_issuer                    = "https://<your_okta_domain>.okta.com/oauth2/<your_auth_server_id>"
  external_oauth_token_user_mapping_claim  = ["email"]
  external_oauth_snowflake_user_mapping_attribute = "EMAIL_ADDRESS"

  # Optional parameters
  external_oauth_any_role_mode             = "ENABLE"
  external_oauth_allowed_roles_list        = ["ACCOUNTADMIN"]
  external_oauth_blocked_roles_list        = ["SYSADMIN"]
  external_oauth_audience_list             = ["https://<your_snowflake_account_url>"]
  external_oauth_jws_keys_url              = ["https://<your_okta_domain>/oauth2/<your_auth_server_id>/v1/keys"]
  comment                                  = "Terraform managed external OAuth integration"

  timeouts {
    create = "10m"
    update = "10m"
    read   = "5m"
    delete = "10m"
  }
}
```

--------------------------------

### Update Snowflake OAuth API Configuration (Terraform)

Source: https://github.REDACTED.md

Demonstrates how to adjust Snowflake provider configuration for OAuth API changes, nesting related fields under 'token_accessor'. This change impacts how authentication details are specified.

```Terraform
provider "snowflake" {
  # before
  browser_auth        = false
  oauth_access_token  = "<access_token>"
  oauth_refresh_token = "<refresh_token>"
  oauth_client_id     = "<client_id>"
  oauth_client_secret = "<client_secret>"
  oauth_endpoint      = "<endpoint>"
  oauth_redirect_url  = "<redirect_uri>"

  # after
  authenticator = "ExternalBrowser"
  token         = "<access_token>"
  token_accessor {
    refresh_token   = "<refresh_token>"
    client_id       = "<client_id>"
    client_secret   = "<client_secret>"
    token_endpoint  = "<endpoint>"
    redirect_uri    = "<redirect_uri>"
  }
}
```

Source: https://github.com/snowflakedb/terraform-provider-snowflake/blob/main/ROADMAP.md

Our roadmap > (2025-08-14) Roadmap update > OAuth2 authorization code and client credential flows: This is the continuation work connected with Snowflake’s goal to have all users strongly authenticated.
We want to provide a wide choice of stronger options from the provider.
The underlying Go driver has been recently [released](https://docs.snowflake.com/en/release-notes/clients-drivers/golang-2025#version-1-14-0-april-30-2025) with support for OAuth2 authorization code and client credential flows.
We want to enable them through the provider. Workload Identity Federation will be supported before the end of 2025.
```

### Configure Snowflake OAuth Access Token via Environment Variables

Source: https://github.com/snowflakedb/terraform-provider-snowflake/blob/main/docs/index.md

Sets environment variables for Snowflake authentication using an OAuth access token. This method requires obtaining a new token when the current one expires.

```bash
export SNOWFLAKE_USER='...'
export SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN='...'
```

--------------------------------

### Correct Snowflake authenticator for token authentication

Source: https://github.REDACTED.md

Fixes an issue where the provider incorrectly defaulted the authenticator to `OAUTH` when the `token` field was used, preventing overrides. Now, the default authenticator can be overridden using configuration or environment variables.

```go
func (p *provider) Configure(ctx context.Context, resp *schema.ConfigureResp) error {
	// ... logic to handle authenticator override ...
}
```

```hcl
provider "snowflake" {
  authenticator = "PROGRAMMATIC_ACCESS_TOKEN"
}
```

```bash
export SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR="PROGRAMMATIC_ACCESS_TOKEN"
```

```toml
authenticator = "PROGRAMMATIC_ACCESS_TOKEN"
```

--------------------------------

### Snowflake Provider Authentication with PAT

Source: https://github.com/snowflakedb/terraform-provider-snowflake/blob/main/docs/guides/authentication_methods.md

This example shows how to configure the Snowflake Terraform provider to authenticate using a Programmatic Access Token (PAT). It specifies the `PROGRAMMATIC_ACCESS_TOKEN` authenticator and passes the token via a variable. This method is suitable for CI/CD pipelines or automated deployments where direct password authentication is not desired.

```terraform
provider "snowflake" {
  organization_name = "<organization_name>"
  account_name      = "<account_name>"
  user              = "<user_name>"
  authenticator     = "PROGRAMMATIC_ACCESS_TOKEN"
  token             = var.token
}

variable "token" {
  type      = string
  sensitive = true
}
```

--------------------------------

### Configure Snowflake JWT Authentication with Private Key

Source: https://github.com/snowflakedb/terraform-provider-snowflake/blob/main/docs/guides/authentication_methods.md

This snippet shows how to configure the Snowflake Terraform provider to use JWT authentication with a private key. It specifies the organization, account, user, authenticator, and the path to the private key file. The private key can be loaded using the `file()` function or sourced from a Terraform variable.

```terraform
provider "snowflake" {
  organization_name = "<organization_name>"
  account_name      = "<account_name>"
  user              = "<user_name>"
  authenticator     = "SNOWFLAKE_JWT"
  private_key       = file("~/.ssh/snowflake_private_key.p8")
  # Optionally, set it with Terraform variable.
  private_key       = var.private_key
}

variable "private_key" {
  type      = string
  sensitive = true
}
```

--------------------------------

### Update Snowflake OAuth API Configuration (Terraform)

Source: https://github.REDACTED.md

Demonstrates how to adjust Snowflake provider configuration for OAuth API changes, nesting related fields under 'token_accessor'. This change impacts how authentication details are specified.

```Terraform
provider "snowflake" {
  # before
  browser_auth        = false
  oauth_access_token  = "<access_token>"
  oauth_refresh_token = "<refresh_token>"
  oauth_client_id     = "<client_id>"
  oauth_client_secret = "<client_secret>"
  oauth_endpoint      = "<endpoint>"
  oauth_redirect_url  = "<redirect_uri>"

  # after
  authenticator = "ExternalBrowser"
  token         = "<access_token>"
  token_accessor {
    refresh_token   = "<refresh_token>"
    client_id       = "<client_id>"
    client_secret   = "<client_secret>"
    token_endpoint  = "<endpoint>"
    redirect_uri    = "<redirect_uri>"
  }
}
```
```

     1→name: Pulumi Preview
     2→
     3→on:
     4→  pull_request:
     5→    paths:
     6→      - "infrastructure/pulumi/**"
     7→      - ".github/workflows/pulumi-preview.yml"
     8→
     9→env:
    10→  CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN }}
    11→  CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID }}
    12→  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: ${{ secrets.R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID }}
    13→  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: ${{ secrets.R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY }}
    14→  PULUMI_CONFIG_PASSPHRASE: ${{ secrets.PULUMI_CONFIG_PASSPHRASE }}
    15→  SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME }}
    16→  SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME }}
    17→  SNOWFLAKE_USER: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_USER }}
    18→  SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY }}
    19→  SNOWFLAKE_ROLE: SYSADMIN
    20→
    21→jobs:
    22→  preview:
    23→    name: Preview
    24→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    25→    permissions:
    26→      contents: read
    27→      pull-requests: write
    28→    defaults:
    29→      run:
    30→        working-directory: infrastructure/pulumi
    31→
    32→    steps:
    33→      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
    34→
    35→      - uses: actions/setup-go@v5
    36→        with:
    37→          go-version: "1.24"
    38→          cache-dependency-path: infrastructure/pulumi/go.sum
    39→
    40→      - name: Install dependencies
    41→        run: go mod tidy
    42→
    43→      - name: Login to R2 backend
    44→        run: pulumi login 's3://pulumi?endpoint=https://${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID }}.r2.cloudflarestorage.com&region=auto&s3ForcePathStyle=true'
    45→
    46→      - name: Select stack
    47→        run: pulumi stack select dev
    48→
    49→      - name: Pulumi Preview
    50→        id: preview
    51→        run: |
    52→          set +e
    53→
    54→          # Run preview with JSON output for parsing
    55→          pulumi preview --json 2>/dev/null > preview.json
    56→          JSON_EXIT=$?
    57→
    58→          # Also get human-readable output
    59→          pulumi preview --diff 2>&1 | tee preview.txt
    60→          PREVIEW_EXIT=${PIPESTATUS[0]}
    61→
    62→          echo "exit_code=$PREVIEW_EXIT" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    63→
    64→          # Parse JSON for accurate counts
    65→          if [ -f preview.json ] && [ -s preview.json ]; then
    66→            CREATES=$(jq '[.steps[]? | select(.op == "create")] | length' preview.json 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    67→            UPDATES=$(jq '[.steps[]? | select(.op == "update")] | length' preview.json 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    68→            DELETES=$(jq '[.steps[]? | select(.op == "delete")] | length' preview.json 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    69→            SAMES=$(jq '[.steps[]? | select(.op == "same")] | length' preview.json 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    70→          else
    71→            # Fallback to text parsing
    72→            CREATES=$(grep -c "^\s*+.*create" preview.txt 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    73→            UPDATES=$(grep -c "^\s*~.*update" preview.txt 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    74→            DELETES=$(grep -c "^\s*-.*delete" preview.txt 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    75→            SAMES=$(grep -oP '\d+(?= unchanged)' preview.txt 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
    76→          fi
    77→
    78→          echo "creates=$CREATES" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    79→          echo "updates=$UPDATES" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    80→          echo "deletes=$DELETES" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    81→          echo "sames=$SAMES" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    82→
    83→          # Determine result status
    84→          TOTAL_CHANGES=$((CREATES + UPDATES + DELETES))
    85→          if [ $PREVIEW_EXIT -ne 0 ]; then
    86→            echo "result=failed" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    87→          elif [ $TOTAL_CHANGES -eq 0 ]; then
    88→            echo "result=no-changes" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    89→          else
    90→            echo "result=has-changes" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    91→          fi
    92→
    93→      - name: Generate PR Comment
    94→        id: comment
    95→        run: |
    96→          RESULT="${{ steps.preview.outputs.result }}"
    97→          CREATES="${{ steps.preview.outputs.creates }}"
    98→          UPDATES="${{ steps.preview.outputs.updates }}"
    99→          DELETES="${{ steps.preview.outputs.deletes }}"
   100→          SAMES="${{ steps.preview.outputs.sames }}"
   101→
   102→          # Set header based on result
   103→          case $RESULT in
   104→            "failed")
   105→              HEADER="## ❌ Pulumi Preview Failed"
   106→              BADGE="https://img.shields.io/badge/pulumi-failed-red?style=flat-square&logo=pulumi"
   107→              ;;
   108→            "no-changes")
   109→              HEADER="## ✅ Pulumi Preview: No Changes"
   110→              BADGE="https://img.shields.io/badge/pulumi-no%20changes-green?style=flat-square&logo=pulumi"
   111→              ;;
   112→            "has-changes")
   113→              HEADER="## 🔄 Pulumi Preview: Changes Detected"
   114→              BADGE="https://img.shields.io/badge/pulumi-changes%20detected-blue?style=flat-square&logo=pulumi"
   115→              ;;
   116→          esac
   117→
   118→          # Build comment
   119→          cat << 'COMMENT_EOF' > comment.md
   120→          HEADER_PLACEHOLDER
   121→
   122→          ![Pulumi](BADGE_PLACEHOLDER)
   123→
   124→          ### 📊 Resource Changes
   125→
   126→          | | Create | Update | Delete | Unchanged |
   127→          |:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|
   128→          | Count | **CREATES_PLACEHOLDER** | **UPDATES_PLACEHOLDER** | **DELETES_PLACEHOLDER** | SAMES_PLACEHOLDER |
   129→          | | 🆕 | 📝 | 🗑️ | ⏸️ |
   130→
   131→          COMMENT_EOF
   132→
   133→          # Add warning section for destructive changes
   134→          if [ "$DELETES" -gt 0 ]; then
   135→            cat << 'WARNING_EOF' >> comment.md
   136→
   137→          > [!CAUTION]
   138→          > **DELETES_PLACEHOLDER resources will be deleted.** Please review carefully before merging.
   139→
   140→          WARNING_EOF
   141→          fi
   142→
   143→          # Add details section
   144→          cat << 'DETAILS_EOF' >> comment.md
   145→
   146→          <details>
   147→          <summary>📋 Preview Details</summary>
   148→
   149→          ```diff
   150→          DETAILS_EOF
   151→
   152→          # Add preview output (truncated if needed)
   153→          head -c 60000 preview.txt >> comment.md
   154→
   155→          cat << 'END_EOF' >> comment.md
   156→          ```
   157→
   158→          </details>
   159→
   160→          ---
   161→
   162→          <sub>🏗️ **Stack:** `dev` | 📁 **Path:** `infrastructure/pulumi` | 🔗 **Workflow:** [Run #${{ github.run_number }}](${{ github.server_url }}/${{ github.repository }}/actions/runs/${{ github.run_id }})</sub>
   163→          END_EOF
   164→
   165→          # Replace placeholders
   166→          sed -i "s|HEADER_PLACEHOLDER|$HEADER|g" comment.md
   167→          sed -i "s|BADGE_PLACEHOLDER|$BADGE|g" comment.md
   168→          sed -i "s|CREATES_PLACEHOLDER|$CREATES|g" comment.md
   169→          sed -i "s|UPDATES_PLACEHOLDER|$UPDATES|g" comment.md
   170→          sed -i "s|DELETES_PLACEHOLDER|$DELETES|g" comment.md
   171→          sed -i "s|SAMES_PLACEHOLDER|$SAMES|g" comment.md
   172→
   173→      - name: Post PR Comment
   174→        uses: actions/github-script@v7
   175→        with:
   176→          script: |
   177→            const fs = require('fs');
   178→            const body = fs.readFileSync('infrastructure/pulumi/comment.md', 'utf8');
   179→
   180→            const marker = '<!-- pulumi-preview-comment -->';
   181→            const fullBody = marker + '\n' + body;
   182→
   183→            // Find existing comment
   184→            const { data: comments } = await github.rest.issues.listComments({
   185→              owner: context.repo.owner,
   186→              repo: context.repo.repo,
   187→              issue_number: context.issue.number,
   188→            });
   189→
   190→            const existingComment = comments.find(c => c.body.includes(marker));
   191→
   192→            if (existingComment) {
   193→              await github.rest.issues.updateComment({
   194→                owner: context.repo.owner,
   195→                repo: context.repo.repo,
   196→                comment_id: existingComment.id,
   197→                body: fullBody
   198→              });
   199→              console.log('Updated existing comment');
   200→            } else {
   201→              await github.rest.issues.createComment({
   202→                owner: context.repo.owner,
   203→                repo: context.repo.repo,
   204→                issue_number: context.issue.number,
   205→                body: fullBody
   206→              });
   207→              console.log('Created new comment');
   208→            }
   209→
   210→      - name: Set exit code
   211→        if: steps.preview.outputs.result == 'failed'
   212→        run: exit 1
   213→
```

> AGENT

コミット完了しました。変更のサマリー:

### CI/CD 変更点

| 項目 | Before (RSA) | After (OIDC) |
|------|-------------|--------------|
| 認証方式 | `SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY` (Secret) | GitHub OIDC トークン (短命) |
| ユーザー | `${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_USER }}` | `PULUMI_SVC` (固定値) |
| ロール | `SYSADMIN` | `PULUMI_ROLE` |
| 必要な Secrets | 4つ (ORG, ACCOUNT, USER, PRIVATE_KEY) | 2つ (ORG, ACCOUNT) |
| environment | なし | `pulumi` |
| permissions | - | `id-token: write` 追加 |

### Snowsight で実行する SQL（OIDC 版）

```sql
-- 1. ロール作成
USE ROLE USERADMIN;
CREATE ROLE IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_ROLE
  COMMENT = 'Role for Pulumi infrastructure management';
GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO ROLE SYSADMIN;

-- 2. サービスユーザー作成（OIDC）
CREATE USER IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_SVC
  TYPE = SERVICE
  WORKLOAD_IDENTITY = (
    TYPE = OIDC
    ISSUER = 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com'
    SUBJECT = 'repo:ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare:environment:pulumi'
  )
  DEFAULT_ROLE = PULUMI_ROLE
  COMMENT = 'Service user for Pulumi IaC (GitHub Actions OIDC)';
GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO USER PULUMI_SVC;

-- 3. 権限付与
USE ROLE SECURITYADMIN;
GRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE WAREHOUSE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
```

### GitHub で必要な作業

1. **Environment `pulumi` を作成** (Settings > Environments)
2. **Secrets を追加** (environment-level or repo-level):
   - `SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME`
   - `SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME`

PR 作成に進みますか？

> DEVELOPER

Snowsight で実行するものは何？

> AGENT

Pulumi が Snowflake に接続するための**ブートストラップ SQL** です。Pulumi 自身が使うユーザー・ロール・権限は、Pulumi では作れない（鶏と卵問題）ので、手動で1回だけ実行する必要があります。

```sql
-- 1. Pulumi 用ロール
USE ROLE USERADMIN;
CREATE ROLE IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_ROLE
  COMMENT = 'Role for Pulumi infrastructure management';
GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO ROLE SYSADMIN;

-- 2. Pulumi 用サービスユーザー（GitHub OIDC 認証）
CREATE USER IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_SVC
  TYPE = SERVICE
  WORKLOAD_IDENTITY = (
    TYPE = OIDC
    ISSUER = 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com'
    SUBJECT = 'repo:ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare:environment:pulumi'
  )
  DEFAULT_ROLE = PULUMI_ROLE
  COMMENT = 'Service user for Pulumi IaC (GitHub Actions OIDC)';
GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO USER PULUMI_SVC;

-- 3. Pulumi に DB・Warehouse を作成する権限を付与
USE ROLE SECURITYADMIN;
GRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE WAREHOUSE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
```

これを Snowsight で実行すると、GitHub Actions から OIDC で Snowflake に接続できるようになり、Pulumi が `RAW` Database や `ANALYTICS_WH` Warehouse を作成できるようになります。

> DEVELOPER

今後データベースやウェアハウス以外も作る可能性がある。

> AGENT

それなら `PULUMI_ROLE` の権限を広げておきましょう。Snowsight の SQL を更新します。

```sql
-- 1. ロール作成
USE ROLE USERADMIN;
CREATE ROLE IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_ROLE
  COMMENT = 'Role for Pulumi infrastructure management';
GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO ROLE SYSADMIN;

-- 2. サービスユーザー作成（OIDC）
CREATE USER IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_SVC
  TYPE = SERVICE
  WORKLOAD_IDENTITY = (
    TYPE = OIDC
    ISSUER = 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com'
    SUBJECT = 'repo:ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare:environment:pulumi'
  )
  DEFAULT_ROLE = PULUMI_ROLE
  COMMENT = 'Service user for Pulumi IaC (GitHub Actions OIDC)';
GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO USER PULUMI_SVC;

-- 3. 権限付与
USE ROLE SECURITYADMIN;
GRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE WAREHOUSE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE ROLE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE USER ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE INTEGRATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
```

追加した権限:

| 権限 | 用途 |
|------|------|
| `CREATE DATABASE` | DB 作成 (既存) |
| `CREATE WAREHOUSE` | WH 作成 (既存) |
| `CREATE ROLE` | ロール管理 (追加) |
| `CREATE USER` | ユーザー管理 (追加) |
| `CREATE INTEGRATION` | ストレージ/API 連携 (追加) |

`ACCOUNTADMIN` は渡さず、必要な権限だけ個別に付与する形にしています。今後さらに必要になったら `GRANT` を追加すれば OK です。

### Grant CREATE LISTING Privilege on Account

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/user-guide/data-exchange-marketplace-privileges

This snippet demonstrates how to grant the global CREATE LISTING privilege on an account to a custom role named 'myrole'. This privilege allows users with the role to create and manage listings and provider profiles. It requires the ACCOUNTADMIN role to execute.

```sql
USE ROLE ACCOUNTADMIN;
GRANT CREATE LISTING ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE myrole;
```

--------------------------------

### Grant Global CREATE LISTING Privilege (SQL)

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/user-guide/data-exchange-marketplace-privileges

Grants the global CREATE LISTING privilege at the account level. This allows the role to create listings or provider profiles in the Snowflake Marketplace or a Data Exchange. Typically granted by ACCOUNTADMIN.

```sql
GRANT CREATE LISTING ON ACCOUNT TO <role_name>;
```

--------------------------------

### Granting Snowflake CREATE LISTING Privilege (SQL)

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/release-notes/bcr-bundles/2025_03/bcr-1926

Demonstrates the SQL syntax for granting the privilege to create listings on an account to a custom role. The 'Before' example uses the older privilege name, while the 'After' example uses the new name effective from the 2025_03 bundle.

```sql
GRANT CREATE DATA EXCHANGE LISTING ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE <role_name>;

```

```sql
GRANT CREATE LISTING ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE <role_name>;

```

--------------------------------

### Account Object Privileges Definition (SQL)

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/sql-reference/sql/grant-privilege-application

Defines the specific privileges that can be granted on various account-level objects like COMPUTE POOL, DATABASE, WAREHOUSE, etc., to an application.

```sql
accountObjectPrivileges ::=
-- For COMPUTE POOL
   { MODIFY | MONITOR | OPERATE | USAGE } [ , ... ]
-- For CONNECTION
   { FAILOVER } [ , ... ]
-- For DATABASE
   { APPLYBUDGET | CREATE { DATABASE ROLE | SCHEMA } 
   | IMPORTED PRIVILEGES | MODIFY | MONITOR | USAGE } [ , ... ]
-- For EXTERNAL VOLUME
   { USAGE } [ , ... ]
-- For FAILOVER GROUP
   { FAILOVER | MODIFY | MONITOR | REPLICATE } [ , ... ]
-- For INTEGRATION
   { USAGE | USE_ANY_ROLE } [ , ... ]
-- For REPLICATION GROUP
   { MODIFY | MONITOR | REPLICATE } [ , ... ]
-- For RESOURCE MONITOR
   { MODIFY | MONITOR } [ , ... ]
-- For USER
   { MONITOR } [ , ... ]
-- For WAREHOUSE
   { APPLYBUDGET | MODIFY | MONITOR | USAGE | OPERATE } [ , ... ]
```

### CREATE LISTING > Access control requirements

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/sql-reference/sql/create-listing

To execute the `CREATE LISTING` operation, the role you are using must have specific privileges. At a minimum, the role needs the `CREATE LISTING` privilege on the account. This privilege is typically granted to the `ACCOUNTADMIN` role by default but can be extended to other roles. If you are modifying the manifest to configure cross-cloud auto-fulfillment, you will also need delegated privileges for that. Additionally, operating on any object within a schema requires at least one privilege on the parent database and one privilege on the parent schema.
```

### Grant Core Privileges for Container Apps - SQL

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/native-apps/tutorials/na-spcs-tutorial

These SQL commands grant essential privileges to the 'tutorial_role' required for creating and managing containerized applications within Snowflake. This includes permissions for integrations, warehouses, databases, application packages, applications, compute pools, and service endpoints.

```sql
GRANT CREATE INTEGRATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE tutorial_role;
GRANT CREATE WAREHOUSE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE tutorial_role;
GRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE tutorial_role;
GRANT CREATE APPLICATION PACKAGE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE tutorial_role;
GRANT CREATE APPLICATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE tutorial_role;
GRANT CREATE COMPUTE POOL ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE tutorial_role WITH GRANT OPTION;
GRANT BIND SERVICE ENDPOINT ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE tutorial_role WITH GRANT OPTION;
```

--------------------------------

### Grant Global Privilege to Snowflake Application (SQL)

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/native-apps/requesting-privs

This SQL command is used by a consumer to grant a specific global privilege, such as CREATE DATABASE, to a Snowflake Native App. This action allows the application to perform the requested operation within the consumer's account after the provider has requested it in the manifest file.

```SQL
GRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO APPLICATION hello_snowflake_app;

```

--------------------------------

### Global Privileges Definition (SQL)

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/sql-reference/sql/grant-privilege-application

Defines the set of global privileges that can be granted on an account level to an application in Snowflake.

```sql
globalPrivileges ::=
  {
      CREATE {
       COMPUTE POOL | DATABASE | WAREHOUSE
      }
      | BIND SERVICE ENDPOINT
      | EXECUTE MANAGED TASK
      | MANAGE WAREHOUSES
      | READ SESSION
  }
  [ , ... ]
```

--------------------------------

### Grant Account Privileges to User

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/sql-reference/sql/grant-privilege-user

Grants global privileges on the account to a user. This includes actions like managing support cases, importing shares, or applying policies. The `ALL [ PRIVILEGES ]` option can be used to grant all available global privileges.

```sql
GRANT { globalPrivileges | ALL [ PRIVILEGES ] } ON ACCOUNT TO [ USER ] <user_name> [ WITH GRANT OPTION ]

-- Example:
GRANT APPLY TAG, MONITOR USAGE ON ACCOUNT TO USER data_analyst;
```

--------------------------------

### Account Object Privileges Definition (SQL)

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/sql-reference/sql/grant-privilege-application

Defines the specific privileges that can be granted on various account-level objects like COMPUTE POOL, DATABASE, WAREHOUSE, etc., to an application.

```sql
accountObjectPrivileges ::=
-- For COMPUTE POOL
   { MODIFY | MONITOR | OPERATE | USAGE } [ , ... ]
-- For CONNECTION
   { FAILOVER } [ , ... ]
-- For DATABASE
   { APPLYBUDGET | CREATE { DATABASE ROLE | SCHEMA } 
   | IMPORTED PRIVILEGES | MODIFY | MONITOR | USAGE } [ , ... ]
-- For EXTERNAL VOLUME
   { USAGE } [ , ... ]
-- For FAILOVER GROUP
   { FAILOVER | MODIFY | MONITOR | REPLICATE } [ , ... ]
-- For INTEGRATION
   { USAGE | USE_ANY_ROLE } [ , ... ]
-- For REPLICATION GROUP
   { MODIFY | MONITOR | REPLICATE } [ , ... ]
-- For RESOURCE MONITOR
   { MODIFY | MONITOR } [ , ... ]
-- For USER
   { MONITOR } [ , ... ]
-- For WAREHOUSE
   { APPLYBUDGET | MODIFY | MONITOR | USAGE | OPERATE } [ , ... ]
```
```

> AGENT

ドキュメントから確認できた `GRANT CREATE ... ON ACCOUNT` の一覧です:

| 権限 | 用途 | PULUMI_ROLE に必要？ |
|------|------|:---:|
| `CREATE DATABASE` | データベース | **付与済** |
| `CREATE WAREHOUSE` | ウェアハウス | **付与済** |
| `CREATE ROLE` | ロール | **付与済** |
| `CREATE USER` | ユーザー | **付与済** |
| `CREATE INTEGRATION` | 外部連携 (S3, API等) | **付与済** |
| `CREATE NETWORK POLICY` | ネットワークポリシー | 要検討 |
| `CREATE SHARE` | データシェアリング | 要検討 |
| `CREATE COMPUTE POOL` | Snowpark Container Services | 不要 |
| `CREATE APPLICATION` | Native App | 不要 |
| `CREATE APPLICATION PACKAGE` | Native App パッケージ | 不要 |
| `CREATE LISTING` | Marketplace リスティング | 不要 |

現実的に使う可能性があるのは **`CREATE NETWORK POLICY`** と **`CREATE SHARE`** くらいです。`COMPUTE POOL` / `APPLICATION` / `LISTING` はこのプロジェクトでは不要でしょう。

`NETWORK POLICY` と `SHARE` も追加しておきますか？それとも現状の5つで十分ですか？

```sql
-- 1. ロール作成
USE ROLE USERADMIN;
CREATE ROLE IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_ROLE
  COMMENT = 'Role for Pulumi infrastructure management';
GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO ROLE SYSADMIN;

-- 2. サービスユーザー作成（OIDC）
CREATE USER IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_SVC
  TYPE = SERVICE
  WORKLOAD_IDENTITY = (
    TYPE = OIDC
    ISSUER = 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com'
    SUBJECT = 'repo:ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare:environment:pulumi'
  )
  DEFAULT_ROLE = PULUMI_ROLE
  COMMENT = 'Service user for Pulumi IaC (GitHub Actions OIDC)';
GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO USER PULUMI_SVC;

-- 3. 権限付与
USE ROLE SECURITYADMIN;
-- 基本リソース
GRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE WAREHOUSE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE ROLE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE USER ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE INTEGRATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE NETWORK POLICY ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE SHARE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
-- Snowpark Container Services / Native App
GRANT CREATE COMPUTE POOL ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE APPLICATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT CREATE APPLICATION PACKAGE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
GRANT BIND SERVICE ENDPOINT ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
```

`BIND SERVICE ENDPOINT` も追加しました。Container Services でサービスを外部公開する際に必要です。

> DEVELOPER

SQL compilation error: Invalid object type 'ACCOUNT' for privilege 'CREATE RESOURCE MONITOR'.

> AGENT

`RESOURCE MONITOR` は `ACCOUNTADMIN` 専用で、他のロールに `CREATE` 権限を委譲できないオブジェクトです。その行は削除してください。

```sql
-- この行は削除
GRANT CREATE RESOURCE MONITOR ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;  -- ← 削除
```

Resource Monitor を Pulumi で管理したい場合は、`PULUMI_SVC` に `ACCOUNTADMIN` ロールを付与する必要がありますが、権限が強すぎるのでおすすめしません。Resource Monitor は Snowsight で手動管理が現実的です。

> AGENT

`snowflake/bootstrap.sql` に移動しました。PR 作成に進めますか？

> DEVELOPER

bootstrap.sql はセキュリティ的に大丈夫？

> AGENT

問題ありません。ファイルの中身を確認すると:

| 内容 | 機密性 |
|------|--------|
| `ISSUER = 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com'` | 公開情報（GitHub の OIDC エンドポイント） |
| `SUBJECT = 'repo:ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare:environment:pulumi'` | リポジトリ名は既に公開 |
| `PULUMI_ROLE`, `PULUMI_SVC` | ロール名・ユーザー名のみ。認証情報ではない |
| `GRANT ... ON ACCOUNT` | 権限の定義。接続情報ではない |

パスワード、秘密鍵、トークン、接続文字列は一切含まれていません。OIDC の設定値（issuer/subject）も公開情報で、攻撃者がこれを知っても GitHub Actions の OIDC トークンを偽造することはできません。

> DEVELOPER

OIDCにおいてなにがプライベートな情報なの

> AGENT

OIDC 認証で秘密にすべきものは **トークンそのもの** だけです。

| 情報 | 秘密？ | 理由 |
|------|:------:|------|
| Issuer URL | 公開 | 誰でも知っている GitHub のエンドポイント |
| Subject (repo名等) | 公開 | 信頼関係の条件。知っていても偽造できない |
| Audience (Snowflake URL) | 公開 | アカウントURL は推測可能 |
| **OIDC トークン (JWT)** | **秘密** | 短命（数分）だが、有効な間は認証に使える |

### なぜ設定値が公開で安全か

OIDC の仕組み:

1. GitHub Actions が **秘密鍵で署名した JWT** を発行（この秘密鍵は GitHub のみが持つ）
2. Snowflake が **GitHub の公開鍵** で署名を検証
3. JWT の `iss`（issuer）と `sub`（subject）が、ユーザー設定と一致するか確認

攻撃者が issuer や subject を知っていても、**GitHub の秘密鍵なしでは有効な JWT を作れない**ので認証は通りません。

### CI ワークフローでの扱い

```yaml
# トークンは GITHUB_ENV 経由で渡すが、add-mask で隠蔽済み
echo "::add-mask::$OIDC_TOKEN"
echo "SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN=$OIDC_TOKEN" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"
```

ログにも表示されず、ジョブ終了後は無効になります。

> DEVELOPER

環境変数何が必要？

> AGENT

CI で必要な Secrets は **2つだけ** です。

| Secret | 値の例 | 用途 |
|--------|--------|------|
| `SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME` | `MYORG` | Snowflake 組織名 |
| `SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME` | `MYACCOUNT` | Snowflake アカウント名 |

以下はワークフローにハードコードしてあるので Secret 不要:

| 環境変数 | 値 | 理由 |
|----------|-----|------|
| `SNOWFLAKE_USER` | `PULUMI_SVC` | 公開情報（ユーザー名） |
| `SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR` | `OAUTH` | 認証方式の指定 |
| `SNOWFLAKE_ROLE` | `PULUMI_ROLE` | ロール名 |
| `SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN` | (自動取得) | OIDC ステップで動的に設定 |

GitHub Settings > Environments で `pulumi` 環境を作成し、上記 2つの Secrets を設定すれば OK です。

### Add OIDC Provider via API

Source: https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflare-docs/blob/production/src/content/docs/cloudflare-one/integrations/identity-providers/generic-oidc.mdx

Make a POST request to the Identity Providers endpoint to add a generic OIDC provider to Cloudflare One.

```APIDOC
## POST /accounts/{account_id}/access/identity_providers

### Description
Adds a new identity provider configuration for the specified account. This endpoint supports various identity provider types, including generic OIDC.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
`/accounts/{account_id}/access/identity_providers`

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
- **account_id** (string) - Required - The unique identifier of the account.

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
- **name** (string) - Required - The name of the identity provider.
- **type** (string) - Required - The type of the identity provider. For generic OIDC, use `"oidc"`.
- **config** (object) - Required - Configuration details specific to the identity provider type.
  - **client_id** (string) - Required - The client ID obtained from your identity provider.
  - **client_secret** (string) - Required - The client secret obtained from your identity provider.
  - **auth_url** (string) - Required - The authorization endpoint URL of your identity provider.
  - **token_url** (string) - Required - The token endpoint URL of your identity provider.
  - **certs_url** (string) - Required - The JWKS URI endpoint of your identity provider for key verification.
  - **pkce_enabled** (boolean) - Optional - Enables Proof of Key Exchange (PKCE) if supported by your IdP.
  - **email_claim_name** (string) - Optional - The name of the claim that contains the user's email address.
  - **claims** (array of strings) - Optional - Custom OIDC claims to include in user identities.
  - **scopes** (array of strings) - Optional - OAuth scopes to request from the identity provider. Defaults to `["openid", "email", "profile"]`.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "name": "Generic OIDC example",
  "type": "oidc",
  "config": {
    "client_id": "<your client id>",
    "client_secret": "<your client secret>",
    "auth_url": "https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/auth",
    "token_url": "https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/token",
    "certs_url": "https://www.googleapis.com/oauth2/v3/certs",
    "pkce_enabled": false,
    "email_claim_name": "email",
    "claims": ["employeeID", "groups"],
    "scopes": ["openid", "email", "profile"]
  }
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **id** (string) - The unique identifier of the created identity provider.
- **name** (string) - The name of the identity provider.
- **type** (string) - The type of the identity provider.
- **config** (object) - The configuration details of the identity provider.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "id": "a1b2c3d4-e5f6-7890-1234-567890abcdef",
  "name": "Generic OIDC example",
  "type": "oidc",
  "config": {
    "client_id": "<your client id>",
    "auth_url": "https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/auth",
    "token_url": "https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/token",
    "certs_url": "https://www.googleapis.com/oauth2/v3/certs",
    "pkce_enabled": false,
    "email_claim_name": "email",
    "claims": ["employeeID", "groups"],
    "scopes": ["openid", "email", "profile"]
  }
}
```
```

### Add Generic OIDC Provider via Cloudflare API

Source: https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflare-docs/blob/production/src/content/docs/cloudflare-one/integrations/identity-providers/generic-oidc.mdx

This snippet demonstrates how to add a generic OIDC identity provider to Cloudflare One using a POST request to the Identity Providers API endpoint. It requires the account ID and OIDC provider configuration details such as client ID, client secret, and various URLs.

```json
{
	"name": "Generic OIDC example",
	"type": "oidc",
	"config": {
		"client_id": "<your client id>",
		"client_secret": "<your client secret>",
		"auth_url": "https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/auth",
		"token_url": "https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/token",
		"certs_url": "https://www.googleapis.com/oauth2/v3/certs",
		"pkce_enabled": false,
		"email_claim_name": "email",
		"claims": ["employeeID", "groups"],
		"scopes": ["openid", "email", "profile"]
	}
}
```

--------------------------------

### Configure Generic OIDC Provider with Terraform

Source: https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflare-docs/blob/production/src/content/docs/cloudflare-one/integrations/identity-providers/generic-oidc.mdx

This Terraform configuration defines a `cloudflare_zero_trust_access_identity_provider` resource for a generic OIDC provider. It includes essential configuration parameters like account ID, name, type, and the OIDC connection details.

```terraform
resource "cloudflare_zero_trust_access_identity_provider" "generic_oidc_example" {
		account_id = var.cloudflare_account_id
		name       = "Generic OIDC example"
		type       = "oidc"
		config 	 = {
			client_id = "<your client id>"
			client_secret = "<your client secret>"
			auth_url = "https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/auth"
			token_url = "https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/token"
			certs_url = "https://www.googleapis.com/oauth2/v3/certs"
			pkce_enabled = false
			email_claim_name = "email"
			claims = ["employeeID", "groups"]
			scopes = ["openid", "email", "profile"]
		}
	}
```

### Generic OIDC

Source: https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflare-docs/blob/production/src/content/docs/cloudflare-one/integrations/identity-providers/generic-oidc.mdx

Cloudflare Access has a generic OpenID Connect (OIDC) connector to help you integrate IdPs not already set in Access. To set this up, you first need to create an application in your identity provider. When creating the client/app, your IdP may request an authorized redirect URI. You should enter the following URL: `https://<your-team-name>.cloudflareaccess.com/cdn-cgi/access/callback`. After that, you will need to copy the Client ID, Client secret, Auth URL, Token URL, and Certificate URL from your IdP. These values can typically be found on your identity provider's OIDC discovery endpoint, sometimes referred to as the "well-known URL".

--------------------------------

### Generic OIDC

Source: https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflare-docs/blob/production/src/content/docs/cloudflare-one/integrations/identity-providers/generic-oidc.mdx

After creating an application in your identity provider and obtaining the necessary credentials, you can add the OIDC provider to Cloudflare One. Navigate to Integrations > Identity providers in Cloudflare One and select Add new identity provider. Choose OpenID Connect, name your provider, and fill in the required fields with the information you gathered. You have the option to enable Proof of Key Exchange (PKCE) if your IdP supports it, which enhances security by performing PKCE on all login attempts. Additionally, you can enable SCIM for user and group synchronization by referring to the relevant documentation. Finally, under Optional configurations, you can enter custom OIDC claims to include additional information in users' identities, which will be available in the user identity endpoint. Once configured, select Save.
```

### POST /accounts/{account_id}/access/service_tokens

Source: https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflare-docs/blob/production/src/content/partials/cloudflare-one/access/create-service-token.mdx

Creates a new service token for Cloudflare Access. This token can be used to authenticate API requests.

```APIDOC
## POST /accounts/{account_id}/access/service_tokens

### Description
Creates a new service token for Cloudflare Access. This token can be used to authenticate API requests.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/accounts/{account_id}/access/service_tokens

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
- **account_id** (string) - Required - The ID of the account for which to create the service token.

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
- **name** (string) - Required - A name for the service token, used for identification in logs and for revocation.
- **duration** (string) - Required - The duration for which the token will be valid (e.g., "8760h" for one year). This sets the expiration date.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "name": "CI/CD token",
  "duration": "8760h"
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **result** (object) - Contains the details of the created service token.
  - **client_id** (string) - The client ID for the service token.
  - **client_secret** (string) - The client secret for the service token. This is only displayed once.
  - **created_at** (string) - The timestamp when the token was created.
  - **expires_at** (string) - The timestamp when the token will expire.
  - **id** (string) - The unique identifier for the service token.
  - **name** (string) - The name of the service token.
  - **updated_at** (string) - The timestamp when the token was last updated.
  - **duration** (string) - The duration for which the token is valid.
  - **client_secret_version** (integer) - The version of the client secret.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "result": {
    "client_id": "88bf3b6d86161464f6509f7219099e57.access",
    "client_secret": "REDACTED",
    "created_at": "2025-09-25T22:26:26Z",
    "expires_at": "2026-09-25T22:26:26Z",
    "id": "3537a672-e4d8-4d89-aab9-26cb622918a1",
    "name": "CI/CD token",
    "updated_at": "2025-09-25T22:26:26Z",
    "duration": "8760h",
    "client_secret_version": 1
  }
}
```

### Error Handling
- **400 Bad Request**: Invalid input parameters.
- **401 Unauthorized**: Authentication failed.
- **404 Not Found**: Account not found.
```

--------------------------------

### Create Service API Token using Cloudflare API

Source: https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflare-docs/blob/production/src/content/docs/ai-search/get-started/api.mdx

This cURL command demonstrates how to create a service API token programmatically. It requires an existing API token with token creation permissions and your account ID. The token created grants access to AI Search Index Engine and Workers R2 Storage Write.

```bash
curl -X POST "https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/user/tokens" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer <API_TOKEN>" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  --data '{
            "name": "AI Search Service API Token",
            "policies": [
              {
                "effect": "allow",
                "resources": {
                  "com.cloudflare.api.account.<ACCOUNT_ID>": "*"
                },
                "permission_groups": [
                  { "id": "9e9b428a0bcd46fd80e580b46a69963c" },
                  { "id": "bf7481a1826f439697cb59a20b22293e" }
                ]
              }
            ]
          }'
```

--------------------------------

### Example Response for Service API Token Creation

Source: https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflare-docs/blob/production/src/content/docs/ai-search/get-started/api.mdx

This JSON object represents the successful response from the Cloudflare API when creating a service API token. It includes the token's ID, name, status, issuance and modification timestamps, and the policies associated with it, including the API key value.

```json
{
	"result": {
		"id": "<CF_API_ID>",
		"name": "AI Search Service API Token",
		"status": "active",
		"issued_on": "2025-12-24T22:14:16Z",
		"modified_on": "2025-12-24T22:14:16Z",
		"last_used_on": null,
		"value": "<CF_API_KEY>",
		"policies": [
			{
				"id": "f56e6d5054e147e09ebe5c514f8a0f93",
				"effect": "allow",
				"resources": { "com.cloudflare.api.account.<ACCOUNT_ID>": "*" },
				"permission_groups": [
					{
						"id": "9e9b428a0bcd46fd80e580b46a69963c",
						"name": "AI Search Index Engine"
					},
					{
						"id": "bf7481a1826f439697cb59a20b22293e",
						"name": "Workers R2 Storage Write"
					}
				]
			}
		]
	},
	"success": true,
	"errors": [],
	"messages": []
}
```

--------------------------------

### Create Cloudflare Service Token for DoH

Source: https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflare-docs/blob/production/src/content/docs/cloudflare-one/networks/resolvers-and-proxies/dns/dns-over-https.mdx

This snippet demonstrates how to create a service token for an account using the Cloudflare API. It requires the account ID and an API token for authentication. The output includes the service token's client ID, client secret, and ID, which are necessary for subsequent steps.

```bash
curl "https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/$ACCOUNT_ID/access/service_tokens" \
--header "Authorization: Bearer $CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN" \
--header "Content-Type: application/json" \
--data '{"name":"ACME Corporation service token"}'
```

--------------------------------

### Register Service Token with AI Search API

Source: https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflare-docs/blob/production/src/content/docs/ai-search/get-started/api.mdx

Registers a service token with AI Search using the provided API endpoint. Requires Cloudflare API credentials and the AI Search API token. Outputs a token ID for future use.

```bash
curl -X POST "https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/<ACCOUNT_ID>/ai-search/tokens" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer <AI_SEARCH_API_TOKEN>" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  --data '{
            "cf_api_id": "<CF_API_ID>",
            "cf_api_key": "<CF_API_KEY>",
            "name": "AI Search Service Token"
          }'
```
```

> AGENT

**Cloudflare API は OIDC 認証に対応していません。** API Token 方式のみです。

| プロバイダー | OIDC 対応 | 認証方式 |
|-------------|:---------:|---------|
| Snowflake | あり | WIF OIDC (GitHub OIDC JWT) |
| Cloudflare | **なし** | API Token (Secret に保存) |
| AWS | あり | `aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials` |
| GCP | あり | `google-github-actions/auth` |

Cloudflare は API Token か Service Token しかサポートしておらず、GitHub OIDC トークンを直接受け入れる仕組みがありません。現状の `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` を Secret に保存する方式がベストプラクティスです。

speed!\n\nこのプルリクエストは、Pulumiを用いたインフラストラクチャ管理にSnowflakeプロバイダーを統合し、データウェアハウス関連リソースのIaC化を推進します。これにより、CloudflareリソースとSnowflakeリソースの管理が構造化され、CI/CDパイプラインにおける認証プロセスが強化されます。\n\n### Highlights\n\n* **Snowflakeプロバイダーの追加**: PulumiにSnowflakeプロバイダーが追加され、データベース、スキーマ、ウェアハウスといったSnowflakeリソースをInfrastructure as Code (IaC) で管理できるようになりました。\n* **コードのモジュール化**: `main.go`ファイルが、プロバイダーごとに`cloudflare.go`と`snowflake.go`に分割され、コードの可読性と保守性が向上しました。\n* **OIDC認証の導入**: CI/CDにおけるSnowflake認証にGitHub Actions OIDC (Workload Identity Federation) が採用され、秘密鍵を保存する必要がなくなりました。\n* **ブートストラップSQLのGit管理**: Snowsightで手動実行するSnowflakeの初期設定用SQLスクリプトが`snowflake/bootstrap.sql`としてGit管理されるようになりました。\n\n<details>\n<summary><b>Changelog</b></summary>\n\n* **infrastructure/pulumi/cloudflare.go**\n    * Cloudflareリソース（D1データベース、R2バケット、Workers KVネームスペース）を作成する関数`createCloudflareResources`が追加されました。\n* **infrastructure/pulumi/go.mod**\n    * Pulumi SDKのバージョンが`v3.217.0`から`v3.220.0`に更新されました。\n    * `github.com/pulumi/pulumi-snowflake/sdk/v2`が依存関係に追加されました。\n    * その他のGoモジュール依存関係が更新されました。\n* **infrastructure/pulumi/main.go**\n    * Cloudflareリソースの作成ロジックが`createCloudflareResources`関数に移動され、`main`関数から直接のCloudflareリソース定義が削除されました。\n    * 新たに`createSnowflakeResources`関数が呼び出され、Snowflakeリソースがプロビジョニングされるようになりました。\n    * CloudflareとSnowflakeのリソースIDおよび名前がPulumiの出力としてエクスポートされるようになりました。\n* **infrastructure/pulumi/snowflake.go**\n    * Snowflakeリソース（データベース、スキーマ、ウェアハウス）を作成する関数`createSnowflakeResources`が追加されました。\n* **snowflake/bootstrap.sql**\n    * SnowflakeのPulumi IaC管理に必要なロール、サービスユーザー、および権限を定義するSQLスクリプトが追加されました。\n    * GitHub Actions OIDC認証用の`PULUMI_SVC`サービスユーザーと`PULUMI_ROLE`ロールの設定が含まれています。\n</details>\n\n<details>\n<summary><b>Ignored Files</b></summary>\n\n*   **Ignored by pattern:** `.github/workflows/**` (2)\n    *   .github/workflows/pulumi-deploy.yml\n    *   .github/workflows/pulumi-preview.yml\n</details>\n\n<details>\n<summary><b>Activity</b></summary>\n\n* =============================================================================\n+-- このスクリプトは Snowsight で手動実行する（Pulumi 自身では作成できない）\n+-- 実行順序: 1. ロール → 2. ユーザー → 3. 権限付与\n+-- =============================================================================\n+\n+-- 1. ロール作成\n+USE ROLE USERADMIN;\n+\n+CREATE ROLE IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_ROLE\n+  COMMENT = 'Role for Pulumi infrastructure management';\n+\n+GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO ROLE SYSADMIN;\n+\n+-- 2. サービスユーザー作成（GitHub Actions OIDC 認証）\n+CREATE USER IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_SVC\n+  TYPE = SERVICE\n+  WORKLOAD_IDENTITY = (\n+    TYPE = OIDC\n+    ISSUER = 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com'\n+    SUBJECT = 'repo:ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare:environment:pulumi'\n+  )\n+  DEFAULT_ROLE = PULUMI_ROLE\n+  COMMENT = 'Service user for Pulumi IaC (GitHub Actions OIDC)';\n+\n+GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO USER PULUMI_SVC;\n+\n+-- 3. 権限付与\n+USE ROLE SECURITYADMIN;\n+\n+-- 基本リソース\n+GRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n+GRANT CREATE WAREHOUSE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n+GRANT CREATE ROLE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n+GRANT CREATE USER ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n+GRANT CREATE INTEGRATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n+GRANT CREATE NETWORK POLICY ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n+GRANT CREATE SHARE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n+\n+-- Snowpark Container Services / Native App\n+GRANT CREATE COMPUTE POOL ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n+GRANT CREATE APPLICATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n+GRANT CREATE APPLICATION PACKAGE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n+GRANT BIND SERVICE ENDPOINT ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE cloudflare.NewWorkersKvNamespace(ctx, \"data-cache\", &cloudflare.WorkersKvNamespaceArgs{\n+\t\tAccountId: pulumi.String(accountId),\n+\t\tTitle:     pulumi.String(\"data-cache\"),\n+\t})\n+\tif err != nil > AGENT

レビュー対応完了しました。

### 対応済み
- OIDC トークンのバリデーション追加（空/"null" チェック + `exit 1`）
- `go.mod` の `// indirect` 修正

### スキップ（理由付き）
- `AutoResume` 型変更 → Provider v2 は `StringPtrInput` 型でコンパイル済み
- 定数化 → Pulumi リソースのインライン値は既存コードと同じパターン
- bootstrap.sql 権限削減 → ユーザーが明示的に広めの権限を選択済み

### CI について
`Invalid OAuth access token` は GitHub Environment `pulumi` と Snowflake ブートストラップが未設定のため想定内です。設定後に再実行すれば通ります。

マージは Snowflake / GitHub 側のセットアップ後にしましょうか？

> DEVELOPER

environment なしにしたい。

> AGENT

environment を外すと、GitHub OIDC の `sub` クレームがトリガーごとに変わります:

- `pull_request` → `repo:ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare:pull_request`
- `push` to main → `repo:ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare:ref:refs/heads/main`

Snowflake は 1 ユーザー 1 subject なので、**ユーザーを 2 つに分ける**必要があります。
