     1→-- =============================================================================
     2→-- Snowflake Bootstrap: Pulumi IaC 用のロール・ユーザー・権限
     3→-- =============================================================================
     4→-- このスクリプトは Snowsight で手動実行する（Pulumi 自身では作成できない）
     5→-- 実行順序: 1. ロール → 2. ユーザー → 3. 権限付与
     6→-- =============================================================================
     7→
     8→-- 1. ロール作成
     9→USE ROLE USERADMIN;
    10→
    11→CREATE ROLE IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_ROLE
    12→  COMMENT = 'Role for Pulumi infrastructure management';
    13→
    14→GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO ROLE SYSADMIN;
    15→
    16→-- 2. サービスユーザー作成（GitHub Actions OIDC 認証）
    17→CREATE USER IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_SVC
    18→  TYPE = SERVICE
    19→  WORKLOAD_IDENTITY = (
    20→    TYPE = OIDC
    21→    ISSUER = 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com'
    22→    SUBJECT = 'repo:ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare:environment:pulumi'
    23→  )
    24→  DEFAULT_ROLE = PULUMI_ROLE
    25→  COMMENT = 'Service user for Pulumi IaC (GitHub Actions OIDC)';
    26→
    27→GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO USER PULUMI_SVC;
    28→
    29→-- 3. 権限付与
    30→USE ROLE SECURITYADMIN;
    31→
    32→-- 基本リソース
    33→GRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    34→GRANT CREATE WAREHOUSE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    35→GRANT CREATE ROLE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    36→GRANT CREATE USER ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    37→GRANT CREATE INTEGRATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    38→GRANT CREATE NETWORK POLICY ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    39→GRANT CREATE SHARE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    40→
    41→-- Snowpark Container Services / Native App
    42→GRANT CREATE COMPUTE POOL ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    43→GRANT CREATE APPLICATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    44→GRANT CREATE APPLICATION PACKAGE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    45→GRANT BIND SERVICE ENDPOINT ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    46→

`Invalid OAuth access token` エラーが出ています。OIDC トークンの取得自体は成功していますが、Snowflake 側での認証が通っていません。

「修正した」とのことですが、何を修正されましたか？以下のどれかが原因の可能性があります:

1. **Snowsight で `bootstrap.sql` は実行済み？** — `PULUMI_SVC` ユーザーが存在するか
2. **GitHub Environment `pulumi` は作成済み？** — OIDC subject が `environment:pulumi` と一致する必要がある
3. **Secrets 設定済み？** — `SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME` と `SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME` が正しいか

> AGENT

同じエラー `Invalid OAuth access token` です。OIDC トークンの取得は成功していますが、Snowflake 側で拒否されています。

考えられる原因:

1. **GitHub Environment `pulumi` が未作成** — Settings > Environments で `pulumi` という名前の environment を作成する必要があります（保護ルール不要、空でOK）。これがないと OIDC の `sub` クレームに `environment:pulumi` が含まれず、Snowflake の SUBJECT と一致しません
2. **audience の不一致** — `SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME` と `SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME` の値が Snowflake アカウント URL と一致しているか

GitHub Environment `pulumi` は作成済みですか？

> DEVELOPER

作った。

> AGENT

OIDC トークン取得は成功しているのに Snowflake が拒否しています。デバッグのため、OIDC トークンの中身（`sub`/`aud` クレーム）を確認するステップを一時的に追加しましょう。

}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ta93abe/Developer/github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare/.github/workflows/pulumi-preview.yml",
  "old_string": "      - name: Get Snowflake OIDC token\n        run: |\n          OIDC_TOKEN=$(curl -sS -H \"Authorization: bearer $ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_TOKEN\" \\\n            \"${ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL}&audience=https://${SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME}-${SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME}.snowflakecomputing.com\" | jq -r '.value')\n          if [ -z \"$OIDC_TOKEN\" ] || [ \"$OIDC_TOKEN\" = \"null\" ]; then\n            echo \"::error::Failed to retrieve Snowflake OIDC token\"\n            exit 1\n          fi\n          echo \"::add-mask::$OIDC_TOKEN\"\n          echo \"SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN=$OIDC_TOKEN\" >> \"$GITHUB_ENV\"",
  "new_string": "      - name: Get Snowflake OIDC token\n        run: |\n          OIDC_TOKEN=$(curl -sS -H \"Authorization: bearer $ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_TOKEN\" \\\n            \"${ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL}&audience=https://${SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME}-${SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME}.snowflakecomputing.com\" | jq -r '.value')\n          if [ -z \"$OIDC_TOKEN\" ] || [ \"$OIDC_TOKEN\" = \"null\" ]; then\n            echo \"::error::Failed to retrieve Snowflake OIDC token\"\n            exit 1\n          fi\n          # Debug: show token claims (sub, aud, iss) without exposing the full token\n          echo \"$OIDC_TOKEN\" | cut -d. -f2 | base64 -d 2>/dev/null | jq '{sub, aud, iss}' || true\n          echo \"::add-mask::$OIDC_TOKEN\"\n          echo \"SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN=$OIDC_TOKEN\" >> \"$GITHUB_ENV\""
}

Now I have all the information needed. Let me compile the comprehensive research findings.

---

## Research Results: Snowflake Workload Identity Federation (WIF) with GitHub Actions OIDC

### Root Cause of Your Error

**The error `390303 (08004): Invalid OAuth access token` is occurring because you are using `SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR=OAUTH` when you should be using `SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR=WORKLOAD_IDENTITY`.** This is the core issue. The `OAUTH` authenticator expects a token obtained through an External OAuth security integration flow, not a raw OIDC JWT from GitHub Actions. The `WORKLOAD_IDENTITY` authenticator is specifically designed for WIF/OIDC authentication.

---

### 1. Does the gosnowflake driver support WIF/OIDC auth? What version is needed?

**Yes.** The Go Snowflake driver (`gosnowflake`) supports Workload Identity Federation:

- **v1.15.0** (July 1, 2025): Added as **Private Preview** (PrPr). Required setting `SF_ENABLE_EXPERIMENTAL_AUTHENTICATION=true`.
- **v1.16.0** (August 14, 2025): Promoted to **General Availability (GA)**. The experimental flag is no longer required.

The driver added:
- `AuthTypeWorkloadIdentityFederation` authenticator constant (string value: `"WORKLOAD_IDENTITY"`)
- `WorkloadIdentityProvider` connection parameter (accepts `"AWS"`, `"GCP"`, `"AZURE"`, or `"OIDC"`)

**Your problem:** The Pulumi Snowflake provider v2.12.0 (from your `go.mod`: `github.com/pulumi/pulumi-snowflake/sdk/v2 v2.12.0`) wraps the Terraform Snowflake provider. The Pulumi provider v2.12.0 **does** list `WORKLOAD_IDENTITY` as a valid authenticator value per the [Pulumi Registry docs](https://www.pulumi.com/registry/packages/snowflake/api-docs/provider/). However, the key question is whether the underlying gosnowflake version bundled with this provider is >= v1.16.0. Since the Pulumi provider v2.12.0 was published on February 13, 2025, which is **before** gosnowflake v1.15.0 (July 2025), there is a timeline mismatch.

**Important caveat:** The Pulumi Snowflake provider SDK (`pulumi-snowflake/sdk/v2`) is the SDK for declaring resources, not the runtime driver. The actual Snowflake connection is made by the Pulumi *provider binary* (downloaded at runtime), which has its own gosnowflake dependency. The provider binary version may be newer than the SDK version. You should check which provider plugin version Pulumi is actually downloading.

---

### 2. Does `WORKLOAD_IDENTITY (TYPE = OIDC)` on a user work with `authenticator=OAUTH`?

**No.** This is the fundamental mismatch causing your error.

- **`authenticator=OAUTH`**: Expects an OAuth access token obtained through Snowflake's **External OAuth** security integration. The token must be issued by an OAuth authorization server that Snowflake trusts via a configured security integration.
- **`authenticator=WORKLOAD_IDENTITY`**: This is the correct authenticator for WIF. When combined with `workloadIdentityProvider=OIDC`, the driver handles OIDC token retrieval and exchange with Snowflake directly.

Your current workflow at `/Users/ta93abe/Developer/github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare/.github/workflows/pulumi-deploy.yml` (line 19) sets:
```yaml
SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR: OAUTH
```

This needs to be changed to:
```yaml
SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR: WORKLOAD_IDENTITY
```

And you also need to add:
```yaml
SNOWFLAKE_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER: OIDC
```

---

### 3. Do we also need a Snowflake Security Integration (EXTERNAL_OAUTH)?

**No.** Workload Identity Federation does **not** require an `EXTERNAL_OAUTH` security integration. The `EXTERNAL_OAUTH` security integration is only needed for the traditional OAuth flow (when using `authenticator=OAUTH`).

With WIF, the trust relationship is established directly on the **user object** via the `WORKLOAD_IDENTITY` parameter:

```sql
CREATE USER PULUMI_SVC
  TYPE = SERVICE
  DEFAULT_ROLE = PULUMI_ROLE
  WORKLOAD_IDENTITY = (
    TYPE = OIDC
    ISSUER = 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com'
    SUBJECT = 'repo:ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare:environment:pulumi'
    OIDC_AUDIENCE_LIST = ('snowflakecomputing.com')
  );
```

Snowflake validates the OIDC JWT directly against the issuer's JWKS endpoint -- no intermediary security integration is needed.

---

### 4. Is there a known issue with the Pulumi Snowflake provider (v2.12.0) and WIF auth?

The Pulumi Snowflake provider v2.12.0 documentation [lists `WORKLOAD_IDENTITY` as a valid authenticator](https://www.pulumi.com/registry/packages/snowflake/api-docs/provider/), along with the `workloadIdentityProvider` configuration parameter (sourced from `SNOWFLAKE_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER` env var).

However, there is a potential issue: the **WIF feature in gosnowflake only reached GA in v1.16.0 (August 14, 2025)**. The Pulumi provider v2.12.0 published Feb 13, 2025, may be using an older gosnowflake version that predates WIF support entirely. If the actual provider plugin binary bundled with Pulumi is from before August 2025, WIF may not work even if the configuration accepts the value.

**Recommendation:** Check which version of the provider plugin Pulumi is actually running. You can see this with `pulumi plugin ls`. If the provider plugin binary uses gosnowflake < v1.15.0, you will need to upgrade it.

---

### 5. What is the correct way to authenticate from GitHub Actions to Snowflake using OIDC?

There are **two approaches**:

#### Approach A: Using Snowflake CLI (Recommended/Simplest)

The [Snowflake CLI action](https://github.com/snowflakedb/snowflake-cli-action) has built-in OIDC support:

```yaml
jobs:
  deploy:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    environment: pulumi
    permissions:
      id-token: write
      contents: read
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Set up Snowflake CLI
        uses: snowflakedb/snowflake-cli-action@v2.0
        with:
          use-oidc: true
          cli-version: "3.14.0"
      - name: Test connection
        env:
          SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT }}
        run: snow connection test -x
```

This approach lets the Snowflake CLI handle all token retrieval and exchange internally.

#### Approach B: Using the Driver Directly (Your Use Case with Pulumi)

For the Pulumi provider, you need to configure the environment variables correctly. The driver (when using `WORKLOAD_IDENTITY` authenticator with `OIDC` provider) will automatically read the `ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL` and `ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_TOKEN` environment variables provided by GitHub Actions to obtain the OIDC token. **You may not need to manually fetch the token at all.**

However, you can also pass the token explicitly. Here is the corrected workflow:

```yaml
env:
  SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME }}
  SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME }}
  SNOWFLAKE_USER: PULUMI_SVC
  SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR: WORKLOAD_IDENTITY
  SNOWFLAKE_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER: OIDC
  SNOWFLAKE_ROLE: PULUMI_ROLE

jobs:
  deploy:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    environment: pulumi
    permissions:
      id-token: write
      contents: read
```

If the driver handles token retrieval automatically (which it should for GitHub Actions when `ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL` and `ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_TOKEN` are available), you can remove the manual OIDC token fetch step entirely. If it does not, you can pass the token via `SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN`.

#### Snowflake User Configuration

The `OIDC_AUDIENCE_LIST` parameter is important. When you manually request a token with a custom audience like `https://ORG-ACCOUNT.snowflakecomputing.com`, the user must be configured to accept that audience. Alternatively, use the standard audience `snowflakecomputing.com`:

```sql
CREATE OR REPLACE USER PULUMI_SVC
  TYPE = SERVICE
  DEFAULT_ROLE = PULUMI_ROLE
  WORKLOAD_IDENTITY = (
    TYPE = OIDC
    ISSUER = 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com'
    SUBJECT = 'repo:ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare:environment:pulumi'
    OIDC_AUDIENCE_LIST = ('snowflakecomputing.com')
  );
GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO USER PULUMI_SVC;
```

And in the workflow, request the token with `audience=snowflakecomputing.com` if you must fetch it manually:
```yaml
- name: Get Snowflake OIDC token
  run: |
    OIDC_TOKEN=$(curl -sS -H "Authorization: bearer $ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_TOKEN" \
      "${ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL}&audience=snowflakecomputing.com" | jq -r '.value')
    echo "::add-mask::$OIDC_TOKEN"
    echo "SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN=$OIDC_TOKEN" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"
```

---

### Summary of Required Changes

| What | Current (Broken) | Correct |
|------|-----------------|---------|
| Authenticator | `SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR=OAUTH` | `SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR=WORKLOAD_IDENTITY` |
| WIF Provider | (not set) | `SNOWFLAKE_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER=OIDC` |
| Security Integration | Not needed | Not needed (confirmed) |
| Manual token fetch | May not be needed | Driver may auto-retrieve via `ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_*` |
| gosnowflake version | Must be >= v1.15.0 (PrPr) or >= v1.16.0 (GA) | Check with `pulumi plugin ls` |
| User OIDC_AUDIENCE_LIST | Verify it matches the `audience` in your token request | Must match |

### Files to Modify

- `/Users/ta93abe/Developer/github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare/.github/workflows/pulumi-deploy.yml` (line 19)
- `/Users/ta93abe/Developer/github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare/.github/workflows/pulumi-preview.yml` (line 18)

In both files, change:
```yaml
SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR: OAUTH
```
to:
```yaml
SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR: WORKLOAD_IDENTITY
SNOWFLAKE_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER: OIDC
```

調査結果が出ました。原因が判明しました。**両方（ワークフロー側とSnowflake側）に修正が必要**な可能性があります。

## 問題の原因

### 1. ワークフロー側: `SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR` が間違っている
- 現在: `OAUTH` → 従来のExternal OAuth用（Security Integration必要）
- 正しい: `WORKLOAD_IDENTITY` → WIF/OIDC用
- さらに `SNOWFLAKE_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER: OIDC` の追加が必要

### 2. Snowflake側: `OIDC_AUDIENCE_LIST` が未設定
- 現在の bootstrap.sql に `OIDC_AUDIENCE_LIST` がない
- トークンの `aud` クレーム（`https://<org>-<account>.snowflakecomputing.com`）を受け入れるには明示的に設定が必要

### 3. Pulumi Provider のバージョン
- gosnowflake ドライバーの WIF 対応は v1.15.0 (2025年7月) から
- Pulumi provider v2.12.0 が十分新しいか確認が必要

両方を修正します。まず現在のファイルを確認して修正します。

   123→            "no-changes")
   124→              HEADER="## ✅ Pulumi Preview: No Changes"
   125→              BADGE="https://img.shields.io/badge/pulumi-no%20changes-green?style=flat-square&logo=pulumi"
   126→              ;;
   127→            "has-changes")
   128→              HEADER="## 🔄 Pulumi Preview: Changes Detected"
   129→              BADGE="https://img.shields.io/badge/pulumi-changes%20detected-blue?style=flat-square&logo=pulumi"
   130→              ;;
   131→          esac
   132→
   133→          # Build comment
   134→          cat << 'COMMENT_EOF' > comment.md
   135→          HEADER_PLACEHOLDER
   136→
   137→          ![Pulumi](BADGE_PLACEHOLDER)
   138→
   139→          ### 📊 Resource Changes
   140→
   141→          | | Create | Update | Delete | Unchanged |
   142→          |:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|
   143→          | Count | **CREATES_PLACEHOLDER** | **UPDATES_PLACEHOLDER** | **DELETES_PLACEHOLDER** | SAMES_PLACEHOLDER |
   144→          | | 🆕 | 📝 | 🗑️ | ⏸️ |
   145→
   146→          COMMENT_EOF
   147→
   148→          # Add warning section for destructive changes
   149→          if [ "$DELETES" -gt 0 ]; then
   150→            cat << 'WARNING_EOF' >> comment.md
   151→
   152→          > [!CAUTION]
   153→          > **DELETES_PLACEHOLDER resources will be deleted.** Please review carefully before merging.
   154→
   155→          WARNING_EOF
   156→          fi
   157→
   158→          # Add details section
   159→          cat << 'DETAILS_EOF' >> comment.md
   160→
   161→          <details>
   162→          <summary>📋 Preview Details</summary>
   163→
   164→          ```diff
   165→          DETAILS_EOF
   166→
   167→          # Add preview output (truncated if needed)
   168→          head -c 60000 preview.txt >> comment.md
   169→
   170→          cat << 'END_EOF' >> comment.md
   171→          ```
   172→
   173→          </details>
   174→
   175→          ---
   176→
   177→          <sub>🏗️ **Stack:** `dev` | 📁 **Path:** `infrastructure/pulumi` | 🔗 **Workflow:** [Run #${{ github.run_number }}](${{ github.server_url }}/${{ github.repository }}/actions/runs/${{ github.run_id }})</sub>
   178→          END_EOF
   179→
   180→          # Replace placeholders
   181→          sed -i "s|HEADER_PLACEHOLDER|$HEADER|g" comment.md
   182→          sed -i "s|BADGE_PLACEHOLDER|$BADGE|g" comment.md
   183→          sed -i "s|CREATES_PLACEHOLDER|$CREATES|g" comment.md
   184→          sed -i "s|UPDATES_PLACEHOLDER|$UPDATES|g" comment.md
   185→          sed -i "s|DELETES_PLACEHOLDER|$DELETES|g" comment.md
   186→          sed -i "s|SAMES_PLACEHOLDER|$SAMES|g" comment.md
   187→
   188→      - name: Post PR Comment
   189→        uses: actions/github-script@v7
   190→        with:
   191→          script: |
   192→            const fs = require('fs');
   193→            const body = fs.readFileSync('infrastructure/pulumi/comment.md', 'utf8');
   194→
   195→            const marker = '<!-- pulumi-preview-comment -->';
   196→            const fullBody = marker + '\n' + body;
   197→
   198→            // Find existing comment
   199→            const { data: comments } = await github.rest.issues.listComments({
   200→              owner: context.repo.owner,
   201→              repo: context.repo.repo,
   202→              issue_number: context.issue.number,
   203→            });
   204→
   205→            const existingComment = comments.find(c => c.body.includes(marker));
   206→
   207→            if (existingComment) {
   208→              await github.rest.issues.updateComment({
   209→                owner: context.repo.owner,
   210→                repo: context.repo.repo,
   211→                comment_id: existingComment.id,
   212→                body: fullBody
   213→              });
   214→              console.log('Updated existing comment');
   215→            } else {
   216→              await github.rest.issues.createComment({
   217→                owner: context.repo.owner,
   218→                repo: context.repo.repo,
   219→                issue_number: context.issue.number,
   220→                body: fullBody
   221→              });
   222→              console.log('Created new comment');
   223→            }
   224→
   225→      - name: Set exit code
   226→        if: steps.preview.outputs.result == 'failed'
   227→        run: exit 1
   228→
```

    48→            "${ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL}&audience=https://${SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME}-${SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME}.snowflakecomputing.com" | jq -r '.value')

     1→-- =============================================================================
     2→-- Snowflake Bootstrap: Pulumi IaC 用のロール・ユーザー・権限
     3→-- =============================================================================
     4→-- このスクリプトは Snowsight で手動実行する（Pulumi 自身では作成できない）
     5→-- 実行順序: 1. ロール → 2. ユーザー → 3. 権限付与
     6→-- =============================================================================
     7→
     8→-- 1. ロール作成
     9→USE ROLE USERADMIN;
    10→
    11→CREATE ROLE IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_ROLE
    12→  COMMENT = 'Role for Pulumi infrastructure management';
    13→
    14→GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO ROLE SYSADMIN;
    15→
    16→-- 2. サービスユーザー作成（GitHub Actions OIDC 認証）
    17→CREATE USER IF NOT EXISTS PULUMI_SVC
    18→  TYPE = SERVICE
    19→  WORKLOAD_IDENTITY = (
    20→    TYPE = OIDC
    21→    ISSUER = 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com'
    22→    SUBJECT = 'repo:ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare:environment:pulumi'
    23→  )
    24→  DEFAULT_ROLE = PULUMI_ROLE
    25→  COMMENT = 'Service user for Pulumi IaC (GitHub Actions OIDC)';
    26→
    27→GRANT ROLE PULUMI_ROLE TO USER PULUMI_SVC;
    28→
    29→-- 3. 権限付与
    30→USE ROLE SECURITYADMIN;
    31→
    32→-- 基本リソース
    33→GRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    34→GRANT CREATE WAREHOUSE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    35→GRANT CREATE ROLE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    36→GRANT CREATE USER ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    37→GRANT CREATE INTEGRATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    38→GRANT CREATE NETWORK POLICY ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    39→GRANT CREATE SHARE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    40→
    41→-- Snowpark Container Services / Native App
    42→GRANT CREATE COMPUTE POOL ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    43→GRANT CREATE APPLICATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    44→GRANT CREATE APPLICATION PACKAGE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    45→GRANT BIND SERVICE ENDPOINT ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;
    46→

> AGENT

エラーメッセージが変わりました！今度はより具体的です：

```
JWT contains an invalid audience ('aud') claim.
The JWT's audience is [https://***-***.snowflakecomputing.com],
but it must be a non-empty subset of the audience list [snowflakecomputing.com].
```

**原因**: Snowflake が期待する audience は `snowflakecomputing.com` なのに、トークンの audience は `https://<org>-<account>.snowflakecomputing.com` になっています。

2箇所を修正する必要があります：

1. **ワークフロー**: OIDC トークン取得時の audience を `snowflakecomputing.com` に変更
2. **または bootstrap.sql**: `OIDC_AUDIENCE_LIST` でフルURLを許可

シンプルな方法（ワークフロー側で audience を合わせる）で修正します。

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01RfY61BR1XscmZwzUfcvPWF
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ta93abe/Developer/github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare/.github/workflows/pulumi-preview.yml",
  "old_string": "          OIDC_TOKEN=$(curl -sS -H \"Authorization: bearer $ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_TOKEN\" \\\n            \"${ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL}&audience=https://${SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME}-${SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME}.snowflakecomputing.com\" | jq -r '.value')",
  "new_string": "          OIDC_TOKEN=$(curl -sS -H \"Authorization: bearer $ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_TOKEN\" \\\n            \"${ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL}&audience=snowflakecomputing.com\" | jq -r '.value')"
}
```

> AGENT

## 原因は2つありました

### 1. `SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR` の値が間違っていた

| | 設定していた値 | 正しい値 |
|---|---|---|
| 認証方式 | `OAUTH` | `WORKLOAD_IDENTITY` |

- `OAUTH` は**External OAuth Security Integration**経由のトークン用（従来方式）
- `WORKLOAD_IDENTITY` は**WIF/OIDC**用（ユーザーの `WORKLOAD_IDENTITY` 設定で直接検証する新方式）
- さらに `SNOWFLAKE_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER: OIDC` の追加も必要だった

これが `390303: Invalid OAuth access token` の原因。Snowflake は OIDC JWT を「OAuth トークン」として処理しようとして拒否していた。

### 2. OIDC トークンの audience が間違っていた

| | 設定していた値 | 正しい値 |
|---|---|---|
| audience | `https://<org>-<account>.snowflakecomputing.com` | `snowflakecomputing.com` |

authenticator を `WORKLOAD_IDENTITY` に修正したら、次のエラーが出ました：
```
JWT's audience is [https://***-***.snowflakecomputing.com],
but it must be a subset of [snowflakecomputing.com]
```

Snowflake のデフォルトの audience list は `snowflakecomputing.com` で、フルURLではなくドメインのみを期待していた。

**CI は現在 Pass しています。** Snowflake の Database, Schema, Warehouse 3リソースの create が Preview で確認できています。

> DEVELOPER

OIDC トークンの audience が間違っていた
どこを変更したの？

> AGENT

**ワークフロー側**（GitHub Actions でトークンを取得するとき）の audience パラメータを変更しました。

両ワークフローの OIDC トークン取得ステップで：

```yaml
# Before
"${ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL}&audience=https://${SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME}-${SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME}.snowflakecomputing.com"

# After
"${ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL}&audience=snowflakecomputing.com"
```

Snowflake 側（bootstrap.sql）は変更なしです。Snowflake のデフォルト audience list が `snowflakecomputing.com` なので、それに合わせてトークン取得時の audience を変更しました。

> DEVELOPER

audienceパラメータってどのファイルで指定してる？

> AGENT

`.github/workflows/pulumi-preview.yml` と `.github/workflows/pulumi-deploy.yml` の2ファイルです。

`Get Snowflake OIDC token` ステップの `curl` コマンドで `&audience=snowflakecomputing.com` として指定しています。

> AGENT

PR #38 のレビュー対応を開始します。まず PR 情報とレビューコメントを確認します。

{"body":"![high](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/high-priority.svg)\n\n`PULUMI_ROLE`に付与されている権限が、現在 Pulumi で管理しているリソース（データベース、スキーマ、ウェアハウス）に対して必要以上に広範です。例えば、`CREATE ROLE`、`CREATE USER`、`CREATE INTEGRATION` などの権限は現時点では使用されていません。最小権限の原則に従い、現在必要な権限のみを付与し、不要な権限はコメントアウトすることをお勧めします。将来的に権限が必要になった時点でコメントを解除する方が安全です。\n\n```sql\n-- 基本リソース\nGRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\nGRANT CREATE WAREHOUSE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-- GRANT CREATE ROLE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-- GRANT CREATE USER ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-- GRANT CREATE INTEGRATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-- GRANT CREATE NETWORK POLICY ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-- GRANT CREATE SHARE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n\n-- Snowpark Container Services / Native App\n-- GRANT CREATE COMPUTE POOL ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-- GRANT CREATE APPLICATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-- GRANT CREATE APPLICATION PACKAGE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-- GRANT BIND SERVICE ENDPOINT ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n```","created_at":"2026-02-15T06:02:59Z","id":2808611450,"line":45,"path":"snowflake/bootstrap.sql","user":"gemini-code-assist[bot]"}

{"body":"_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_\n\n**Add error handling for the OIDC token retrieval.**\n\nIf the curl request fails or the response doesn't contain `.value`, `OIDC_TOKEN` will be `\"null\"` or empty, and the workflow will proceed with an invalid `SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN`, causing a confusing auth failure later in `pulumi up`.\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e🛡️ Proposed fix\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n```diff\n       - name: Get Snowflake OIDC token\n         run: |\n           OIDC_TOKEN=$(curl -sS -H \"Authorization: bearer $ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_TOKEN\" \\\n             \"${ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL}\u0026audience=https://${SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME}-${SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME}.snowflakecomputing.com\" | jq -r '.value')\n+          if [ -z \"$OIDC_TOKEN\" ] || [ \"$OIDC_TOKEN\" = \"null\" ]; then\n+            echo \"::error::Failed to retrieve Snowflake OIDC token\"\n+            exit 1\n+          fi\n           echo \"::add-mask::$OIDC_TOKEN\"\n           echo \"SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN=$OIDC_TOKEN\" \u003e\u003e \"$GITHUB_ENV\"\n```\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- suggestion_start --\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e📝 Committable suggestion\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n\u003e ‼️ **IMPORTANT**\n\u003e Carefully review the code before committing. Ensure that it accurately replaces the highlighted code, contains no missing lines, and has no issues with indentation. {"body":"_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_\n\n**Overly broad privileges for current requirements — consider least-privilege.**\n\nThe PR objective states Pulumi will manage only three resources: a Database, a Schema, and a Warehouse. Only `CREATE DATABASE` and `CREATE WAREHOUSE` are needed for that (schema creation is covered by database ownership). The remaining grants — `CREATE ROLE`, `CREATE USER`, `CREATE INTEGRATION`, `CREATE NETWORK POLICY`, `CREATE SHARE`, plus all Snowpark/Native App privileges on Lines 42-45 — are unused and widen the blast radius if the service principal is compromised.\n\nConsider granting only what's needed now and adding privileges incrementally as new Pulumi resources are introduced.\n\n\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e🔒 Proposed minimal privilege set\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n```diff\n -- 基本リソース\n GRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n GRANT CREATE WAREHOUSE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-GRANT CREATE ROLE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-GRANT CREATE USER ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-GRANT CREATE INTEGRATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-GRANT CREATE NETWORK POLICY ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-GRANT CREATE SHARE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-\n--- Snowpark Container Services / Native App\n-GRANT CREATE COMPUTE POOL ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-GRANT CREATE APPLICATION ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-GRANT CREATE APPLICATION PACKAGE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n-GRANT BIND SERVICE ENDPOINT ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n```\n\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- suggestion_start --\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e📝 Committable suggestion\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n\u003e ‼️ **IMPORTANT**\n\u003e Carefully review the code before committing. Ensure that it accurately replaces the highlighted code, contains no missing lines, and has no issues with indentation. Thoroughly test \u0026 benchmark the code to ensure it meets the requirements.\n\n```suggestion\n-- 基本リソース\nGRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\nGRANT CREATE WAREHOUSE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE;\n```\n\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- suggestion_end --\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e🤖 Prompt for AI Agents\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n```\nIn `@snowflake/bootstrap.sql` around lines 32 - 45, The current SQL grants include\nmany unused high-privilege statements; restrict to least-privilege by keeping\nonly GRANT CREATE DATABASE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE; and GRANT CREATE\nWAREHOUSE ON ACCOUNT TO ROLE PULUMI_ROLE; and remove or comment out the other\ngrants (GRANT CREATE ROLE, CREATE USER, CREATE INTEGRATION, CREATE NETWORK\nPOLICY, CREATE SHARE, and the Snowpark/Native App grants: CREATE COMPUTE POOL,\nCREATE APPLICATION, CREATE APPLICATION PACKAGE, BIND SERVICE ENDPOINT) so\nPulumi’s service role only has permissions required to manage the\nDatabase/Schema/Warehouse, and add additional grants later when new\nPulumi-managed resources require them.\n```\n\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- fingerprinting:phantom:poseidon:phoenix --\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- This is an auto-generated comment by CodeRabbit --\u003e","created_at":"2026-02-15T06:35:41Z","id":2808645237,"line":45,"path":"snowflake/bootstrap.sql","user":"coderabbitai[bot]"}
```

{"body":"\u003c!-- This is an auto-generated comment: summarize by coderabbit.ai --\u003e\n\u003c!-- walkthrough_start --\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e📝 Walkthrough\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n## Walkthrough\n\nAdds Snowflake OIDC support to GitHub Actions, introduces Pulumi Go modules to provision Cloudflare and Snowflake resources, refactors the Pulumi main to use the new modules, updates Pulumi Go dependencies (including pulumi-snowflake), and adds a Snowflake bootstrap SQL script for service user and role setup.\n\n## Changes\n\n|Cohort / File(s)|Summary|\n|---|---|\n|**GitHub Actions Workflows** \u003cbr\u003e ` .github/workflows/pulumi-deploy.yml`, ` .github/workflows/pulumi-preview.yml`|Add Snowflake env vars and workflow `environment: pulumi`; grant `id-token: write`; insert a \"Get Snowflake OIDC token\" step that exchanges the Actions ID token for a Snowflake OIDC token, masks it, and exports `SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN`.|\n|**Pulumi Cloudflare module** \u003cbr\u003e `infrastructure/pulumi/cloudflare.go`|New file: adds `CloudflareOutputs` and `createCloudflareResources(ctx, accountId)` to import/create D1 database, create R2 bucket, and create Workers KV namespace; returns IDs/names.|\n|**Pulumi Snowflake module** \u003cbr\u003e `infrastructure/pulumi/snowflake.go`|New file: adds `SnowflakeOutputs` and `createSnowflakeResources(ctx)` to create a Snowflake Database, Schema, and Warehouse; returns resource names.|\n|**Pulumi main entry** \u003cbr\u003e `infrastructure/pulumi/main.go`|Refactor: replace inline Cloudflare/Snowflake resource creation with calls to `createCloudflareResources` and `createSnowflakeResources`; wire new outputs (Cloudflare and Snowflake outputs) for stack export.|\n|**Pulumi dependencies** \u003cbr\u003e `infrastructure/pulumi/go.mod`|Update module graph: add `pulumi/pulumi-snowflake/sdk/v2`, bump `pulumi/sdk/v3`, and update various indirect dependencies and versions.|\n|**Snowflake bootstrap script** \u003cbr\u003e `snowflake/bootstrap.sql`|New SQL: creates `PULUMI_ROLE` and `PULUMI_SVC` service user with GitHub Actions OIDC identity, grants account-scoped privileges and Snowpark-related permissions required for Pulumi-managed Snowflake resources.|\n\n## Sequence Diagram\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    participant GitHub as GitHub Actions\n    participant OIDC as OIDC Provider\n    participant Snowflake as Snowflake\n    participant Pulumi as Pulumi Runtime\n    participant Cloudflare as Cloudflare\n\n    GitHub-\u003e\u003eOIDC: request Actions ID token\n    OIDC--\u003e\u003eGitHub: ID token\n    GitHub-\u003e\u003eSnowflake: POST /session/authenticate (exchange ID token, audience=snowflakecomputing.com)\n    Snowflake--\u003e\u003eGitHub: SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN (masked)\n    GitHub-\u003e\u003eGitHub: export SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN env\n    GitHub-\u003e\u003ePulumi: run pulumi preview/up with SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN\n    Pulumi-\u003e\u003eCloudflare: create/import D1 DB, R2 bucket, KV namespace\n    Cloudflare--\u003e\u003ePulumi: return resource IDs/names\n    Pulumi-\u003e\u003eSnowflake: create Database, Schema, Warehouse\n    Snowflake--\u003e\u003ePulumi: return resource names\n    Pulumi--\u003e\u003eGitHub: export stack outputs\n```\n\n## Estimated code review effort\n\n🎯 3 (Moderate) | ⏱️ ~25 minutes\n\n## Poem\n\n\u003e 🐰  \n\u003e I hopped to swap a tiny key,  \n\u003e Snowflake, Cloud, and Pulumi, whee!  \n\u003e Buckets, DBs, a schema bright,  \n\u003e Tokens masked in runner light,  \n\u003e Carrot deploy — all set to be.\n\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- walkthrough_end --\u003e\n\n\n\u003c!-- pre_merge_checks_walkthrough_start --\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e🚥 Pre-merge checks | ✅ 3 | ❌ 1\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails open=\"\"\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e❌ Failed {"body":"## Summary of Changes\n\nHello @ta93abe, I'm Gemini Code Assist[^1]! I'm currently reviewing this pull request and will post my feedback shortly. In the meantime, here's a summary to help you and other reviewers quickly get up to speed!\n\nこのプルリクエストは、Pulumiを用いたインフラストラクチャ管理にSnowflakeプロバイダーを統合し、データウェアハウス関連リソースのIaC化を推進します。これにより、CloudflareリソースとSnowflakeリソースの管理が構造化され、CI/CDパイプラインにおける認証プロセスが強化されます。\n\n### Highlights\n\n* **Snowflakeプロバイダーの追加**: PulumiにSnowflakeプロバイダーが追加され、データベース、スキーマ、ウェアハウスといったSnowflakeリソースをInfrastructure as Code (IaC) で管理できるようになりました。\n* **コードのモジュール化**: `main.go`ファイルが、プロバイダーごとに`cloudflare.go`と`snowflake.go`に分割され、コードの可読性と保守性が向上しました。\n* **OIDC認証の導入**: CI/CDにおけるSnowflake認証にGitHub Actions OIDC (Workload Identity Federation) が採用され、秘密鍵を保存する必要がなくなりました。\n* **ブートストラップSQLのGit管理**: Snowsightで手動実行するSnowflakeの初期設定用SQLスクリプトが`snowflake/bootstrap.sql`としてGit管理されるようになりました。\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e\u003cb\u003eChangelog\u003c/b\u003e\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n* **infrastructure/pulumi/cloudflare.go**\n    * Cloudflareリソース（D1データベース、R2バケット、Workers KVネームスペース）を作成する関数`createCloudflareResources`が追加されました。\n* **infrastructure/pulumi/go.mod**\n    * Pulumi SDKのバージョンが`v3.217.0`から`v3.220.0`に更新されました。\n    * `github.com/pulumi/pulumi-snowflake/sdk/v2`が依存関係に追加されました。\n    * その他のGoモジュール依存関係が更新されました。\n* **infrastructure/pulumi/main.go**\n    * Cloudflareリソースの作成ロジックが`createCloudflareResources`関数に移動され、`main`関数から直接のCloudflareリソース定義が削除されました。\n    * 新たに`createSnowflakeResources`関数が呼び出され、Snowflakeリソースがプロビジョニングされるようになりました。\n    * CloudflareとSnowflakeのリソースIDおよび名前がPulumiの出力としてエクスポートされるようになりました。\n* **infrastructure/pulumi/snowflake.go**\n    * Snowflakeリソース（データベース、スキーマ、ウェアハウス）を作成する関数`createSnowflakeResources`が追加されました。\n* **snowflake/bootstrap.sql**\n    * SnowflakeのPulumi IaC管理に必要なロール、サービスユーザー、および権限を定義するSQLスクリプトが追加されました。\n    * GitHub Actions OIDC認証用の`PULUMI_SVC`サービスユーザーと`PULUMI_ROLE`ロールの設定が含まれています。\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e\u003cb\u003eIgnored Files\u003c/b\u003e\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n*   **Ignored by pattern:** `.github/workflows/**` (2)\n    *   .github/workflows/pulumi-deploy.yml\n    *   .github/workflows/pulumi-preview.yml\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e\u003cb\u003eActivity\u003c/b\u003e\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n* このプルリクエストには、現時点でのレビューコメントや承認などの活動は記録されていません。\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e\u003cb\u003eUsing Gemini Code Assist\u003c/b\u003e\u003c/summary\u003e\n\u003cbr\u003e\n\nThe full guide for Gemini Code Assist can be found on our [documentation page](https://developers.google.com/gemini-code-assist/docs/review-github-code), here are some quick tips.\n\n\u003cb\u003eInvoking Gemini\u003c/b\u003e\n\nYou can request assistance from Gemini at any point by creating a comment using either `/gemini \u003ccommand\u003e` or `@gemini-code-assist \u003ccommand\u003e`. Below is a summary of the supported commands on the current page.\n\nFeature | Command | Description\n--- | --- | ---\nCode Review | `/gemini review` | Performs a code review for the current pull request in its current state.\nPull Request Summary | `/gemini summary` | Provides a summary of the current pull request in its current state.\nComment | @gemini-code-assist | Responds in comments when explicitly tagged, both in pull request comments and review comments.\nHelp | `/gemini help` | Displays a list of available commands.\n\n\u003cb\u003eCustomization\u003c/b\u003e\n\nTo customize Gemini Code Assist for GitHub experience, repository maintainers can create a configuration file and/or provide a custom code review style guide (such as PEP-8 for Python) by creating and adding files to a `.gemini/` folder in the base of the repository. Detailed instructions can be found [here](https://developers.google.com/gemini-code-assist/docs/customize-gemini-behavior-github).\n\n\u003cb\u003eLimitations \u0026 Feedback\u003c/b\u003e\n\nGemini Code Assist may make mistakes. Please leave feedback on any instances where its feedback is incorrect or counter productive. You can react with :thumbsup: and :thumbsdown: on @gemini-code-assist comments. If you're interested in giving your feedback about your experience with Gemini Code Assist for Github and other Google products, sign up [here](https://google.qualtrics.com/jfe/form/SV_2cyuGuTWsEw84yG).\n\n\u003cb\u003eYou can also get AI-powered code generation, chat, as well as code reviews directly in the IDE at no cost with the [Gemini Code Assist IDE Extension](https://cloud.google.com/products/gemini/code-assist).\u003c/b\u003e\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\n[^1]: Review the [Privacy Notices](https://policies.google.com/privacy), [Generative AI Prohibited Use Policy](https://policies.google.com/terms/generative-ai/use-policy), [Terms of Service](https://policies.google.com/terms), and learn how to configure Gemini Code Assist in GitHub [here](https://developers.google.com/gemini-code-assist/docs/customize-gemini-behavior-github). Gemini can make mistakes, so double check it and [use code with caution](https://support.google.com/legal/answer/13505487).\n","created_at":"2026-02-15T06:01:39Z","id":3903384226,"user":"gemini-code-assist[bot]"}

レビューコメントを分析しました。

## レビューコメント対応状況

### 既に対応済み
| コメント | 対応 |
|---------|------|
| CodeRabbit: OIDC トークンバリデーション | 以前のコミットで対応済み |
| CodeRabbit/Copilot: go.mod indirect | `go mod tidy` で対応済み |

### スキップ（設計判断）
| コメント | 理由 |
|---------|------|
| Gemini: `AutoResume` を `pulumi.Bool(true)` に | Provider v2 は `StringPtrInput` 型。Preview 出力で `"true"` が正しく動作している |
| Gemini/CodeRabbit: bootstrap.sql の最小権限 | ユーザーが明示的に Snowpark/Native App 用の権限も追加を要求 |
| Gemini: 定数の抽出 | 値が少数で変更頻度も低い。過剰な抽象化 |

## 完了サマリー

**TA-307: Snowflake リソースを Pulumi で管理する** が完了しました。

### 変更内容
- Pulumi に Snowflake プロバイダーを追加（Database RAW, Schema INGESTION, Warehouse ANALYTICS_WH）
- `main.go` をプロバイダー別に `cloudflare.go` / `snowflake.go` に分割
- GitHub Actions OIDC (WIF) による Snowflake 認証を実装
- `snowflake/bootstrap.sql` で初期設定を Git 管理

### 修正した OIDC 認証の問題
1. `SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR`: `OAUTH` → `WORKLOAD_IDENTITY`
2. `SNOWFLAKE_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER: OIDC` を追加
3. OIDC audience: `https://<org>-<account>.snowflakecomputing.com` → `snowflakecomputing.com`

Excellent! Now I have all the information needed. Let me compile the findings:

## Pulumi Snowflake Setup Exploration - Findings

### 1. **Snowflake Resource Patterns** (`infrastructure/pulumi/snowflake.go`)

The current setup uses a clean, function-based pattern with the following structure:

**Naming Conventions:**
- Pulumi logical names: lowercase, descriptive (e.g., `"raw"`, `"ingestion"`, `"analytics"`)
- Snowflake resource names: UPPERCASE (e.g., `"RAW"`, `"INGESTION"`, `"ANALYTICS_WH"`)

**Resources Created:**
1. **Database** - `RAW` database
   - Created with `snowflake.NewDatabase()`
   - Uses Pulumi name "raw"

2. **Schema** - `INGESTION` schema
   - Created with `snowflake.NewSchema()`
   - References the database via `sfDatabase.Name`
   - Uses Pulumi name "ingestion"

3. **Warehouse** - `ANALYTICS_WH` warehouse
   - Created with `snowflake.NewWarehouse()`
   - Configuration:
     - Size: `XSMALL`
     - Auto-suspend: 60 seconds
     - Auto-resume: enabled
   - Uses Pulumi name "analytics"

**Return Pattern:**
- Returns a `SnowflakeOutputs` struct containing string outputs for database, schema, and warehouse names
- All outputs are `pulumi.StringOutput` types for downstream use in Pulumi

### 2. **Integration with main.go**

The resources are instantiated in `main()` function:
```go
sf, err := createSnowflakeResources(ctx)
```

The outputs are exported at the stack level:
```go
ctx.Export("snowflakeDatabaseName", sf.DatabaseName)
ctx.Export("snowflakeSchemaName", sf.SchemaName)
ctx.Export("snowflakeWarehouseName", sf.WarehouseName)
```

### 3. **Snowflake Directory Structure**

Currently only contains:
- `/snowflake/bootstrap.sql` - One file

The bootstrap.sql file is NOT auto-executed by Pulumi (requires manual execution in Snowsight).

### 4. **Bootstrap Setup** (`snowflake/bootstrap.sql`)

**Purpose:** Sets up authentication for Pulumi to manage Snowflake via GitHub Actions OIDC

**Key Components:**
1. **PULUMI_ROLE** - Custom role for infrastructure management
   - Granted to SYSADMIN
   - Receives specific permissions

2. **PULUMI_SVC** - Service user
   - Type: SERVICE (not regular user)
   - Authentication: GitHub Actions OIDC
   - Issuer: `https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com`
   - Subject: `repo:ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare:environment:pulumi`
   - Default role: PULUMI_ROLE

3. **Permissions Granted to PULUMI_ROLE:**
   - Basic: `CREATE DATABASE`, `CREATE WAREHOUSE`, `CREATE ROLE`, `CREATE USER`
   - Integration: `CREATE INTEGRATION`, `CREATE NETWORK POLICY`, `CREATE SHARE`
   - Advanced: `CREATE COMPUTE POOL`, `CREATE APPLICATION`, `CREATE APPLICATION PACKAGE`, `BIND SERVICE ENDPOINT`

### 5. **Snowflake Provider Version**

From `infrastructure/pulumi/go.mod`:
```
github.com/pulumi/pulumi-snowflake/sdk/v2 v2.12.0
```

**Provider Details:**
- Major version: v2
- Current version: v2.12.0
- Pulumi SDK: v3.220.0
- Cloudflare SDK: v5.49.1 (for reference)
- Go version: 1.24.0

### Summary of Current Patterns

**Strengths:**
- Clean separation of Snowflake resource creation in dedicated function
- Proper error handling with early returns
- Output struct pattern for returning multiple resource references
- GitHub Actions OIDC authentication (secure, no key management)
- Bootstrap script documents manual steps needed

**Current Limitations:**
- Only 3 basic resources (database, schema, warehouse)
- Bootstrap requires manual execution (not automated)
- Limited schema definition - no tables, roles, or other objects defined via Pulumi
- No data integration setup (no storage integration, stages, etc.)

Here is a comprehensive research summary of the Snowflake Git Integration feature.

---

## 1. How Snowflake Git Integration Works

Snowflake Git Integration allows you to connect a remote Git repository (via HTTPS) to Snowflake, creating a local clone (called a "repository stage") that synchronizes files from the remote. This enables you to manage SQL scripts, Python handlers, and other files in Git and execute them directly within Snowflake.

The architecture involves **three Snowflake objects** created in sequence:

1. **Secret** (optional, for authenticated repos) -- stores credentials
2. **API Integration** -- defines the Git HTTPS API provider and allowed URL prefixes
3. **Git Repository** -- the actual repository clone pointing to your remote origin

Once created, the repository appears as a stage (`@repo_name/branches/main/...`) from which you can list files, execute SQL, and import Python handlers.

**Sources:**
- [Using a Git repository in Snowflake](https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/git/git-overview)
- [Setting up Snowflake to use Git](https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/git/git-setting-up)

---

## 2. Authentication Methods

### Option A: No Authentication (Public Repositories)

```sql
CREATE OR REPLACE API INTEGRATION git_api_integration
  API_PROVIDER = git_https_api
  API_ALLOWED_PREFIXES = ('https://github.com/ta93abe')
  ENABLED = TRUE;
```

No secret is needed. Suitable for public GitHub repos.

### Option B: Personal Access Token (PAT) -- Recommended for private repos

```sql
-- Step 1: Create a secret with GitHub PAT
CREATE OR REPLACE SECRET my_db.my_schema.git_secret
  TYPE = password
  USERNAME = 'ta93abe'
  PASSWORD=[REDACTED]';  -- GitHub PAT

-- Step 2: Create API integration referencing the secret
CREATE OR REPLACE API INTEGRATION git_api_integration
  API_PROVIDER = git_https_api
  API_ALLOWED_PREFIXES = ('https://github.com/ta93abe')
  ALLOWED_AUTHENTICATION_SECRETS = (my_db.my_schema.git_secret)
  ENABLED = TRUE;
```

Snowflake documentation recommends using a PAT (not your actual GitHub password) as the `PASSWORD` value.

### Option C: OAuth2 (Snowflake GitHub App)

```sql
CREATE OR REPLACE API INTEGRATION git_api_integration
  API_PROVIDER = git_https_api
  API_ALLOWED_PREFIXES = ('https://github.com')
  API_USER_AUTHENTICATION = (TYPE = SNOWFLAKE_GITHUB_APP)
  ENABLED = TRUE;
```

This uses the Snowflake-managed GitHub App for OAuth2 authentication. Each user authenticates individually.

### Option D: Generic OAuth2 (GitLab, Bitbucket, etc.)

```sql
CREATE OR REPLACE API INTEGRATION git_api_integration
  API_PROVIDER = git_https_api
  API_ALLOWED_PREFIXES = ('https://example.com/my_account')
  API_USER_AUTHENTICATION = (
    TYPE = OAUTH2
    OAUTH_AUTHORIZATION_ENDPOINT = '<endpoint>'
    OAUTH_TOKEN_ENDPOINT = '<token_endpoint>'
    OAUTH_CLIENT_ID = '<client_id>'
    OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET = '<secret>'
    OAUTH_ACCESS_TOKEN_VALIDITY = 3600
    OAUTH_REFRESH_TOKEN_VALIDITY = 2592000
    OAUTH_ALLOWED_SCOPES = ('read_api', 'read_repository')
  )
  ENABLED = TRUE;
```

**Sources:**
- [Setting up Snowflake to use Git](https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/git/git-setting-up)
- [CREATE GIT REPOSITORY](https://docs.snowflake.com/en/sql-reference/sql/create-git-repository)

---

## 3. Snowflake Objects Required

For your use case (`ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare`, private repo, SQL files in `snowflake/` directory), you need to create these objects:

```sql
-- 1. Secret (for private repo authentication)
USE ROLE ACCOUNTADMIN;

CREATE OR REPLACE SECRET admin_db.public.github_pat_secret
  TYPE = password
  USERNAME = 'ta93abe'
  PASSWORD = '<your-github-pat>';

-- 2. API Integration
CREATE OR REPLACE API INTEGRATION github_api_integration
  API_PROVIDER = git_https_api
  API_ALLOWED_PREFIXES = ('https://github.com/ta93abe')
  ALLOWED_AUTHENTICATION_SECRETS = (admin_db.public.github_pat_secret)
  ENABLED = TRUE;

-- 3. Grant privileges to a role
GRANT USAGE ON INTEGRATION github_api_integration TO ROLE SYSADMIN;
GRANT USAGE ON SECRET admin_db.public.github_pat_secret TO ROLE SYSADMIN;
GRANT CREATE GIT REPOSITORY ON SCHEMA my_db.my_schema TO ROLE SYSADMIN;

-- 4. Git Repository
USE ROLE SYSADMIN;

CREATE OR REPLACE GIT REPOSITORY my_db.my_schema.data_engineering_repo
  API_INTEGRATION = github_api_integration
  GIT_CREDENTIALS = admin_db.public.github_pat_secret
  ORIGIN = 'https://github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare.git';
```

**Required Privileges Summary:**

| Privilege | Object | Who Needs It |
|-----------|--------|-------------|
| `CREATE GIT REPOSITORY` | Schema | Role creating the repo |
| `USAGE` | API Integration | Role creating the repo |
| `USAGE` | Secret | Role creating the repo |
| `USAGE` on database/schema | Database & Schema | Role creating the repo |

**Sources:**
- [CREATE GIT REPOSITORY](https://docs.snowflake.com/en/sql-reference/sql/create-git-repository)

---

## 4. Pulumi Snowflake Provider Support

### `snowflake.GitRepository` -- Available (Stable as of v2.12.0)

The Pulumi Snowflake provider (v2.12.0, published February 13, 2026) includes a **stable** `GitRepository` resource.

**Go example:**

```go
import "github.com/pulumi/pulumi-snowflake/sdk/v2/go/snowflake"

repo, err := snowflake.NewGitRepository(ctx, "dataEngineeringRepo", &snowflake.GitRepositoryArgs{
    Database:       pulumi.String("MY_DB"),
    Schema:         pulumi.String("MY_SCHEMA"),
    Name:           pulumi.String("DATA_ENGINEERING_REPO"),
    Origin:         pulumi.String("https://github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare.git"),
    ApiIntegration: pulumi.String("GITHUB_API_INTEGRATION"),
    GitCredentials: pulumi.String("\"ADMIN_DB\".\"PUBLIC\".\"GITHUB_PAT_SECRET\""),
})
```

**Input Properties:**
- `database` (required) -- Target database
- `schema` (required) -- Target schema
- `name` (required) -- Repository identifier
- `origin` (required) -- HTTPS URL of the remote repository
- `api_integration` (required) -- Name of the API integration
- `git_credentials` (optional) -- Fully qualified secret name for auth
- `comment` (optional) -- Description

**Output Properties:**
- `id`, `fully_qualified_name`
- `describe_outputs` -- Result of DESCRIBE GIT REPOSITORY
- `show_outputs` -- Result of SHOW GIT REPOSITORIES

### `snowflake.ApiIntegration` -- Limitation for Git

The `snowflake.ApiIntegration` resource exists but **does NOT currently support `git_https_api` as an API provider**. The documentation explicitly states: *"Currently, API integrations for `git_https_api` API provider are not supported. It will be added in the future."*

This means you would need to create the API Integration via SQL (using `snowflake.UnsafeExecute` or manually) and then reference it by name in the `GitRepository` resource. Similarly, the Secret object may need to be created via SQL or using the appropriate Snowflake secret resource if available.

**Workaround in Pulumi (Go):**

```go
// Create API Integration via raw SQL (since git_https_api is not supported in ApiIntegration resource)
apiIntegration, err := snowflake.NewUnsafeExecute(ctx, "gitApiIntegration", &snowflake.UnsafeExecuteArgs{
    Execute: pulumi.String(`CREATE OR REPLACE API INTEGRATION github_api_integration
        API_PROVIDER = git_https_api
        API_ALLOWED_PREFIXES = ('https://github.com/ta93abe')
        ALLOWED_AUTHENTICATION_SECRETS = (admin_db.public.github_pat_secret)
        ENABLED = TRUE`),
    Revert: pulumi.String(`DROP INTEGRATION IF EXISTS github_api_integration`),
})

// Then create GitRepository referencing it
repo, err := snowflake.NewGitRepository(ctx, "dataEngineeringRepo", &snowflake.GitRepositoryArgs{
    Database:       pulumi.String("MY_DB"),
    Schema:         pulumi.String("MY_SCHEMA"),
    Name:           pulumi.String("DATA_ENGINEERING_REPO"),
    Origin:         pulumi.String("https://github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare.git"),
    ApiIntegration: pulumi.String("GITHUB_API_INTEGRATION"),
    GitCredentials: pulumi.String("\"ADMIN_DB\".\"PUBLIC\".\"GITHUB_PAT_SECRET\""),
}, pulumi.DependsOn([]pulumi.Resource{apiIntegration}))
```

**Sources:**
- [snowflake.GitRepository - Pulumi Registry](https://www.pulumi.com/registry/packages/snowflake/api-docs/gitrepository/)
- [snowflake.ApiIntegration - Pulumi Registry](https://www.pulumi.com/registry/packages/snowflake/api-docs/apiintegration/)
- [snowflake_git_repository - Terraform Registry](https://registry.terraform.io/providers/snowflakedb/snowflake/latest/docs/resources/git_repository)

---

## 5. Running SQL Files from a Git Repository

Once the Git repository is created, you can use these operations:

### Fetch latest changes

```sql
ALTER GIT REPOSITORY my_db.my_schema.data_engineering_repo FETCH;
```

### List available branches and tags

```sql
SHOW GIT BRANCHES IN my_db.my_schema.data_engineering_repo;
SHOW GIT TAGS IN my_db.my_schema.data_engineering_repo;
```

### List files in the repo

```sql
-- List all files in main branch
LS @my_db.my_schema.data_engineering_repo/branches/main/;

-- List SQL files in the snowflake/ directory
LS @my_db.my_schema.data_engineering_repo/branches/main/snowflake/;
```

### Execute a single SQL file

```sql
EXECUTE IMMEDIATE FROM @my_db.my_schema.data_engineering_repo/branches/main/snowflake/create_tables.sql;
```

### Execute with a specific tag/commit

```sql
-- By tag
EXECUTE IMMEDIATE FROM @my_db.my_schema.data_engineering_repo/tags/v1.0.0/snowflake/create_tables.sql;

-- By commit hash
EXECUTE IMMEDIATE FROM @my_db.my_schema.data_engineering_repo/commits/abc123/snowflake/create_tables.sql;
```

### Use Snowflake CLI to execute all SQL files in a directory

```bash
snow git execute @my_db.my_schema.data_engineering_repo/branches/main/snowflake/
```

This command automatically finds all `.sql` and `.py` files and runs `EXECUTE IMMEDIATE` on each.

### Use Git repository files as stored procedure/function handlers

```sql
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE my_procedure()
  RETURNS VARCHAR
  LANGUAGE PYTHON
  RUNTIME_VERSION = '3.12'
  PACKAGES = ('snowflake-snowpark-python')
  IMPORTS = ('@my_db.my_schema.data_engineering_repo/branches/main/python/handler.py')
  HANDLER = 'handler.main';
```

**Sources:**
- [Git operations in Snowflake](https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/git/git-operations)
- [Examples of using Git with Snowflake](https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/git/git-examples)
- [snow git execute CLI](https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/snowflake-cli/command-reference/git-commands/execute)
- [A deep dive into Snowflake's Git Integration](https://select.dev/posts/snowflake-git-integration)

---

## Summary for Your Use Case

For the repo `ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare` with SQL files in `snowflake/`:

| Aspect | Recommendation |
|--------|---------------|
| **Auth method** | GitHub PAT (simplest for private repos) |
| **Objects needed** | Secret + API Integration + Git Repository (3 objects) |
| **Pulumi GitRepository** | Stable in v2.12.0, fully usable |
| **Pulumi ApiIntegration** | Does NOT support `git_https_api` yet; use `UnsafeExecute` as workaround |
| **Pulumi Secret** | May also need `UnsafeExecute` for the Snowflake secret |
| **Running SQL** | `EXECUTE IMMEDIATE FROM @repo/branches/main/snowflake/file.sql` |
| **Batch execution** | `snow git execute` CLI command for all files in a directory |

The main gap in Pulumi support is that the `ApiIntegration` resource does not yet support the `git_https_api` provider type. You will need to use `snowflake.UnsafeExecute` (raw SQL execution) in Pulumi to create the API Integration and Secret, then use the native `snowflake.GitRepository` resource for the repository itself.

### CREATE API INTEGRATION for Git Repository (GitHub App)

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/sql-reference/sql/create-api-integration

Creates a new API integration object for a Git repository using GitHub App authentication or replaces an existing one. Specifies allowed prefixes and enablement status.

```APIDOC
## CREATE API INTEGRATION for Git Repository (GitHub App)

### Description
Creates a new API integration object for a Git repository using GitHub App authentication or replaces an existing one. Specifies allowed prefixes and enablement status.

### Method
CREATE API INTEGRATION

### Endpoint
N/A (This is a DDL command)

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
None

### Request Example
```sql
CREATE OR REPLACE API INTEGRATION my_github_app_integration
  API_PROVIDER = git_https_api
  API_ALLOWED_PREFIXES = ('https://github.com/my-org/')
  API_USER_AUTHENTICATION = (
    TYPE = snowflake_github_app
  )
  ENABLED = TRUE
  COMMENT = 'Integration with my GitHub App'
;
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
None (DDL commands do not return a response body, but indicate success via execution status).

#### Response Example
None
```

### API Integration for Git Repository

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/sql-reference/sql/create-api-integration

This section outlines the parameters used when creating an API integration for connecting to a remote Git repository via `git_https_api`.

```APIDOC
## CREATE API INTEGRATION for Git Repository

### Description
Configures an API integration to connect to a remote Git repository by setting the integration's `API_PROVIDER` parameter to `git_https_api`.

### Method
CREATE API INTEGRATION

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
None

### Parameters for Git Repository Integration:

- **`ALLOWED_AUTHENTICATION_SECRETS`** (tuple of strings | 'all' | 'none') - Required/Optional - Specifies the secrets that UDF or procedure handler code can use when accessing the Git repository. Can be a list of secret names, 'all' (default), or 'none'.

- **`API_USER_AUTHENTICATION`** (object) - Required/Optional - Specifies security integration settings for an OAuth 2.0 flow. The value `snowflake_github_app` specifies OAuth 2.0 as the authentication type for Snowflake to use to connect to the repository. Supported only for repositories hosted at `github.com`.
  - **`TYPE`** (string) - Must be `snowflake_github_app`.

- **`TLS_TRUSTED_CERTIFICATES`** (tuple of strings) - Required/Optional - Specifies secrets containing self-signed certificates for authenticating with a Git repository server over a private link. Secrets must be of type generic string with Base64-encoded certificate data.

- **`USE_PRIVATELINK_ENDPOINT`** (boolean) - Required/Optional - Specifies whether the API integration is used exclusively for connecting to a remote Git repository over an outbound private link. Must be `FALSE` (default) for public Git servers.

### Access Control Requirements
- **Privilege**: `CREATE INTEGRATION`
- **Object**: Account
- **Notes**: Only the `ACCOUNTADMIN` role has this privilege by default. Can be granted to additional roles.

### Usage Notes
- Roles with `OWNERSHIP` or `USAGE` privileges on the API integration can use it directly.
- An API integration is tied to a specific cloud platform account and role, but not a specific HTTPS proxy URL.
- Multiple API integrations can exist for different cloud platform accounts.
- Multiple external functions can use the same API integration.
- Avoid entering personal, sensitive, or regulated data as metadata.
- `OR REPLACE` and `IF NOT EXISTS` clauses are mutually exclusive.
- `CREATE OR REPLACE` statements are atomic.

### Request Example
```sql
CREATE OR REPLACE API INTEGRATION git_repo_api_integration
  API_PROVIDER = git_https_api
  API_USER_AUTHENTICATION = (TYPE = snowflake_github_app)
  ALLOWED_AUTHENTICATION_SECRETS = ('my_git_secret')
  ENABLED = TRUE;
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **`integration_name`** (string) - The name of the created API integration.
- **`integration_type`** (string) - The type of the integration (e.g., `API`).

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "integration_name": "git_repo_api_integration",
  "integration_type": "API"
}
```
```

--------------------------------

### Create API Integration for Git Repository (Snowflake SQL) - Token/OAuth

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/sql-reference/sql/create-api-integration

This SQL syntax creates or replaces an API integration for a Git repository using HTTPS. It supports authentication via personal access tokens or OAuth (for github.com). Parameters include allowed/blocked prefixes, allowed authentication secrets, and enabled status. PrivateLink endpoint and TLS certificates are also configurable.

```sql
CREATE [ OR REPLACE ] API INTEGRATION [ IF NOT EXISTS ] <integration_name>
  API_PROVIDER = git_https_api
  API_ALLOWED_PREFIXES = ('<...>')
  [ API_BLOCKED_PREFIXES = ('<...') ]
  [ ALLOWED_AUTHENTICATION_SECRETS = ( { <secret_name> [, <secret_name>, ... ] } ) | all | none ]
  ENABLED = { TRUE | FALSE }
  [ COMMENT = '<string_literal>' ]
  ;
```

```sql
CREATE [ OR REPLACE ] API INTEGRATION [ IF NOT EXISTS ] <integration_name>
  API_PROVIDER = git_https_api
  API_ALLOWED_PREFIXES = ('https://github.com/<...>')
  [ API_BLOCKED_PREFIXES = ('<...') ]
  API_USER_AUTHENTICATION = (
    TYPE = snowflake_github_app
  )
  ENABLED = { TRUE | FALSE }
  [ COMMENT = '<string_literal>' ]
  ;
```

```sql
CREATE [ OR REPLACE ] API INTEGRATION [ IF NOT EXISTS ] <integration_name>
  API_PROVIDER = git_https_api
  API_ALLOWED_PREFIXES = ('<...>')
  [ API_BLOCKED_PREFIXES = ('<...') ]
  [ ALLOWED_AUTHENTICATION_SECRETS = ( { <secret_name> [, <secret_name>, ... ] } ) | all | none ]
  USE_PRIVATELINK_ENDPOINT = { TRUE | FALSE }
  [ TLS_TRUSTED_CERTIFICATES = ( { <secret_name> [, <secret_name>, ... ] } ) ]
  ENABLED = { TRUE | FALSE }
  [ COMMENT = '<string_literal>' ]
  ;
```

### Execute SQL Code from a Snowflake Git Repository

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/git/git-operations

This SQL command executes code from a specified file within a Git repository clone in Snowflake. It requires the EXECUTE IMMEDIATE FROM privilege. The command points to a specific file path within a branch or tag of the repository.

```sql
EXECUTE IMMEDIATE FROM @snowflake_extensions/branches/main/sql/create-database.sql;
```

--------------------------------

### Create and Execute Snowflake Setup Script from Git

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/git/git-examples

This snippet demonstrates creating a SQL script to set up roles and users in Snowflake, committing it to a Git repository, refreshing the Git repository clone in Snowflake, and then executing the script remotely. Ensure the executing role has necessary privileges.

```sql
CREATE ROLE analyst;

CREATE USER gladys;

GRANT ROLE analyst TO USER gladys;

SHOW GRANTS TO USER gladys;

```

```bash
git add scripts/setup.sql
git commit -m "Adding code to set up new accounts"
git push

```

```sql
ALTER GIT REPOSITORY configuration_repo FETCH;

```

```sql
EXECUTE IMMEDIATE FROM @configuration_repo/branches/main/scripts/setup.sql;

```

--------------------------------

### Execute SQL from Git Repository in Snowflake

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/builders/devops

Allows execution of SQL scripts stored in a remote Git repository directly within Snowflake. Supports Jinja2 templating for parameterization, enabling dynamic script generation and execution.

```sql
EXECUTE IMMEDIATE FROM 'git@github.com:your-repo/your-project.git/path/to/your/script.sql';
```

### Use a Git repository clone file to configure new accounts

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/git/git-examples

This example uses the EXECUTE IMMEDIATE FROM command to execute the SQL statements contained in a file in the Git repository clone. With EXECUTE IMMEDIATE FROM, you can execute (from any Snowflake session) scripts you manage in your remote Git repository. For example, you might have a script that sets up every new Snowflake account in your organization. The script might contain statements to create users, roles, objects, and grant privileges on the account and objects.

--------------------------------

### Working with Git repositories in Snowflake > Execute code from a repository

Source: https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/git/git-operations

You can execute code contained within a file from a Git repository in Snowflake. To perform these operations, you’ll need the appropriate Snowflake access. You can execute code by using either Snowsight or SQL. The `EXECUTE IMMEDIATE FROM` command allows you to execute code in a Git repository clone. For example, you can execute code in a specific SQL file from a particular branch of your cloned repository.
```

```
     1→# TA-307: Snowflake リソースを Pulumi で管理する
     2→
     3→## Context
     4→
     5→既存の Pulumi プロジェクト（Go）は Cloudflare リソース（D1, R2, KV）を管理している。Snowflake をデータウェアハウスとして活用するため、同じ Pulumi プロジェクトに Snowflake プロバイダーを追加し、基盤リソース（Database, Schema, Warehouse）を IaC で管理する。Phase 1 として基本リソースのみ対応。
     6→
     7→## 変更対象ファイル
     8→
     9→| ファイル | 変更内容 |
    10→|---------|---------|
    11→| `infrastructure/pulumi/go.mod` | `pulumi-snowflake/sdk/v2` 依存追加 |
    12→| `infrastructure/pulumi/main.go` | Snowflake リソース定義追加（Database, Schema, Warehouse） |
    13→| `.github/workflows/pulumi-preview.yml` | Snowflake 認証用環境変数追加 |
    14→| `.github/workflows/pulumi-deploy.yml` | Snowflake 認証用環境変数追加 |
    15→
    16→## 実装手順
    17→
    18→### Step 1: ブランチ作成
    19→
    20→```bash
    21→git checkout main && git pull
    22→git checkout -b feat/add-snowflake-provider
    23→```
    24→
    25→### Step 2: Snowflake SDK 依存追加
    26→
    27→```bash
    28→cd infrastructure/pulumi
    29→go get github.com/pulumi/pulumi-snowflake/sdk/v2
    30→go mod tidy
    31→```
    32→
    33→### Step 3: main.go に Snowflake リソース追加
    34→
    35→既存のコードパターン（コメントブロック区切り、error-return-early、ctx.Export）に準拠して以下を追加:
    36→
    37→- **import**: `"github.com/pulumi/pulumi-snowflake/sdk/v2/go/snowflake"` 追加
    38→- **Database**: `RAW` - データ取り込み用
    39→- **Schema**: `RAW.INGESTION` - sfDatabase.Name を参照して依存関係を明示
    40→- **Warehouse**: `ANALYTICS_WH` - XSMALL, auto_suspend=60s, auto_resume=true
    41→- **Outputs**: `snowflakeDatabaseName`, `snowflakeSchemaName`, `snowflakeWarehouseName`
    42→
    43→認証方式: 環境変数による暗黙的プロバイダー構成（Cloudflare プロバイダーと同じパターン）
    44→
    45→### Step 4: CI/CD ワークフロー更新
    46→
    47→両ワークフローの `env:` ブロックに以下を追加:
    48→
    49→```yaml
    50→SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME }}
    51→SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME }}
    52→SNOWFLAKE_USER: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_USER }}
    53→SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY }}
    54→SNOWFLAKE_ROLE: SYSADMIN
    55→```
    56→
    57→### Step 5: ビルド確認
    58→
    59→```bash
    60→cd infrastructure/pulumi && go build ./...
    61→```
    62→
    63→## 設計判断
    64→
    65→- **認証**: JWT (Private Key) - 環境変数で注入。CI/CD との親和性が高い
    66→- **ロール**: `SYSADMIN` - DB/Schema/Warehouse 作成に必要十分な権限
    67→- **Pulumi logical name**: Cloudflare の `"raw"` と Snowflake の `"raw"` は URN にリソースタイプが含まれるため衝突しない
    68→- **`AutoResume`**: Snowflake Provider v2 では `pulumi.StringPtrInput` 型 → `pulumi.String("true")` を使用
    69→
    70→## ユーザー手動作業（PR マージ後）
    71→
    72→1. GitHub Secrets に `SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME`, `SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME`, `SNOWFLAKE_USER`, `SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY` を追加
    73→2. Snowflake 側で RSA キーペア認証を設定
    74→3. ローカルの `.env.local` にも Snowflake 環境変数を追加
    75→
    76→## 検証方法
    77→
    78→1. `go build ./...` でコンパイル確認
    79→2. ローカルで `pulumi preview` 実行 → 3 リソース（Database, Schema, Warehouse）の create が表示されること
    80→3. PR 作成後、CI の Pulumi Preview が正常に実行されること
    81→
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/ta93abe/.claude/plans/wobbly-discovering-kurzweil.md",
  "content": "# Snowflake Git Integration を Pulumi で管理する\n\n## Context\n\n`snowflake/*.sql` を Snowflake から直接参照・実行できるようにしたい。Snowflake の Git Integration 機能を使い、GitHub リポジトリを Snowflake 内のリポジトリステージとして登録する。リポジトリは public なので認証（Secret）は不要。\n\nPulumi Snowflake Provider v2.12.0 の制約:\n- `snowflake.GitRepository` → ネイティブリソースとして利用可能\n- `snowflake.ApiIntegration` → `git_https_api` プロバイダー**未対応**（ドキュメントに明記）\n- → API Integration は `snowflake.UnsafeExecute` で生SQL実行が必要\n\n## 変更対象ファイル\n\n| ファイル | 変更内容 |\n|---------|---------|\n| `infrastructure/pulumi/snowflake.go` | Database `ADMIN`、API Integration（UnsafeExecute）、Git Repository 追加 |\n| `infrastructure/pulumi/main.go` | 新しい Output（`snowflakeGitRepositoryName`）をエクスポート |\n\n## 実装手順\n\n### Step 1: ブランチ作成\n\n```bash\ngit checkout main && git pull\ngit checkout -b feat/add-snowflake-git-integration\n```\n\n### Step 2: snowflake.go にリソース追加\n\n既存の `createSnowflakeResources()` に以下を追加:\n\n#### 2a. Database `ADMIN`\n\nGit Repository の配置先。ツール・管理系リソース用のデータベース。\n\n```go\nadminDb, err := snowflake.NewDatabase(ctx, \"admin\", &snowflake.DatabaseArgs{\n    Name: pulumi.String(\"ADMIN\"),\n})\n```\n\n#### 2b. API Integration（UnsafeExecute）\n\n`git_https_api` は ApiIntegration リソース未対応のため UnsafeExecute で作成。\n\n```go\ngitApiIntegration, err := snowflake.NewUnsafeExecute(ctx, \"gitApiIntegration\", &snowflake.UnsafeExecuteArgs{\n    Execute: pulumi.String(\"CREATE OR REPLACE API INTEGRATION git_api_integration API_PROVIDER = git_https_api API_ALLOWED_PREFIXES = ('https://github.com/ta93abe') ENABLED = TRUE\"),\n    Revert:  pulumi.String(\"DROP INTEGRATION IF EXISTS git_api_integration\"),\n})\n```\n\n#### 2c. Git Repository\n\n```go\ngitRepo, err := snowflake.NewGitRepository(ctx, \"dataEngineering\", &snowflake.GitRepositoryArgs{\n    Database:       adminDb.Name,\n    Schema:         pulumi.String(\"PUBLIC\"),\n    Name:           pulumi.String(\"DATA_ENGINEERING\"),\n    Origin:         pulumi.String(\"https://github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare.git\"),\n    ApiIntegration: pulumi.String(\"GIT_API_INTEGRATION\"),\n}, pulumi.DependsOn([]pulumi.Resource{gitApiIntegration, adminDb}))\n```\n\n#### 2d. SnowflakeOutputs に追加\n\n```go\ntype SnowflakeOutputs struct {\n    DatabaseName      pulumi.StringOutput\n    SchemaName        pulumi.StringOutput\n    WarehouseName     pulumi.StringOutput\n    AdminDatabaseName pulumi.StringOutput  // 追加\n    GitRepositoryName pulumi.StringOutput  // 追加\n}\n```\n\n### Step 3: main.go に Output 追加\n\n```go\nctx.Export(\"snowflakeAdminDatabaseName\", sf.AdminDatabaseName)\nctx.Export(\"snowflakeGitRepositoryName\", sf.GitRepositoryName)\n```\n\n### Step 4: ビルド確認\n\n```bash\ncd infrastructure/pulumi && go build ./...\n```\n\n## 設計判断\n\n- **Database `ADMIN`**: Git Repository は特定のDB.Schemaに属する必要がある。RAW（データ用）とは分離し、管理ツール系の専用DBを作成\n- **Schema `PUBLIC`**: ADMIN DBのデフォルトスキーマで十分\n- **UnsafeExecute**: ApiIntegration が `git_https_api` 未対応のため唯一の手段。Execute/Revert で冪等性を確保\n- **認証不要**: リポジトリが public のため Secret なし、`GIT_CREDENTIALS` パラメータ省略\n- **DependsOn**: Git Repository は API Integration と ADMIN DB に依存\n\n## 使い方（デプロイ後）\n\n```sql\n-- リポジトリを最新に同期\nALTER GIT REPOSITORY ADMIN.PUBLIC.DATA_ENGINEERING FETCH;\n\n-- ファイル一覧\nLS @ADMIN.PUBLIC.DATA_ENGINEERING/branches/main/snowflake/;\n\n-- SQL 実行\nEXECUTE IMMEDIATE FROM @ADMIN.PUBLIC.REDACTED.sql;\n```\n\n## 検証方法\n\n1. `go build ./...` でコンパイル確認\n2. PR 作成 → CI の Pulumi Preview で +3 リソース（Database ADMIN, UnsafeExecute, GitRepository）の create が表示されること\n3. マージ後、Snowsight で `LS @ADMIN.PUBLIC.DATA_ENGINEERING/branches/main/snowflake/` を実行し `bootstrap.sql` が見えること\n"
}
```
